\wtag\ Delivered as \path{Event_Budgeted_Quartic_Certificates_Package.zip} (batch~82, archive~08; arrival \texttt{db37d18c8}), dated 3~October 2026; files placed by \texttt{7d2b1b245} with the prefix \path{20-event-budget-} (Appendix~\ref{fpa:app:provenance}). Report~20 certifies the frozen \emph{ordered} rule of Report~15's compiler (Part~\ref{fpa:part:one}), whose exact finite-support evaluation is Report~19's Theorem~\ref{fpa:lz:thm:main} (Part~\ref{fpa:part:four}); its universal instance is Report~17's source (Part~\ref{fpa:part:three}). Its arithmetic gadgets are those of ``Report~8'', which is source~13 (Part~IV) of \path{signal-machine-collision-certificates} (SMC); the bundled excerpts \path{report8-core.tex} and \path{report8-semilinearity.tex} are SMC's own text and are not shipped, and the circuit-to-quartic layer is credited to \path{canonical-diophantine-certificates} (CDC) in the note after Lemma~\ref{fpa:eb:lem:primitives}. Report~28 (Part~\ref{fpa:part:six}) uses the same compiler pattern for a different rule and a different theorem (note in Section~\ref{fpa:pq:sec:slots}). The Hilbert's-tenth programme's scoped intake \path{Computability/HilbertTenthProblem/Papers/research-wip/native-stream-queue/review_event_budget_clean_clock_intake.md} (commit \texttt{8aabb1453}) read Report~20's complete proof without executing its Python, identified no new defect within its stated theorem scope, and records that the witness arity grows with $n$, $T$, $K$ and the source, so the construction gives no fixed-arity representation and no ordinary-input loader for Report~16's source (Section~\ref{fpa:sec:relation}). The delivered table of contents is omitted, and Report~20's $H_*$ is printed $\mathrm{Ht}_*$ (Section~\ref{fpa:eb:sec:bits}).

\section{Result and implementation boundary}\label{fpa:rep:20:first}
A finite binary configuration is represented by a strictly increasing tuple $X=(x_1,\ldots,x_n)$ of integer occupied sites, with zeros elsewhere. Coordinates are unbounded in both directions. Fix a source accepted by the frozen compiler of the earlier construction. Its ordered factors are $g_0,\ldots,g_{F-1}$ and one literal CA step is
\[
\CA(X)=g_{F-1}\bigl(\cdots g_1(g_0(X))\cdots\bigr).
\]
Report 19 proves that the pinned lazy evaluator computes this exact product on every finite support, including malformed configurations \cite{fpa:r19}. This report changes the certificate representation, not the rule or its factor order.

A \emph{changing factor} is an application of $g_i$ whose simultaneous output differs from the entire pre-factor support. Several separated active occurrences of one factor count as one changing factor. Let $k_T(X)$ be the total number of such applications during the literal evaluation of $T$ CA steps. This number is determined by the input and the ordered product; it is not a guessed event count.

\begin{theorem}[Finite event-budgeted canonical natural fiber]\label{fpa:eb:thm:main}
Fix an accepted finite source, exact mass $n$, horizon $T\in\N$, and budget $K\in\N$. Initial coordinates are supplied as $2n$ natural inputs
\[
x_i=x_i^+-x_i^-,\qquad x_i^+x_i^-=0,\qquad x_1<\cdots<x_n.
\]
There is an effectively generated integer polynomial
\[
P(X^+,X^-,w)=\sum_{j=1}^{E}\rho_j(X^+,X^-,w)^2,
\qquad \deg\rho_j\leq2,
\]
with the following properties for every such fixed admissible input.
\begin{enumerate}
\item If $k_T(X)\leq K$, its natural auxiliary fiber contains exactly one point. With a prescribed sorted endpoint $Y$, that point exists exactly when also $\CA^T(X)=Y$.
\item Otherwise the natural auxiliary fiber is empty.
\item The witness determines every circuit wire, all tested snapshots and factor effects, each actual changing factor, each step-completion absence check, and all idle padding.
\item No auxiliary array or lookup table is indexed by all $F$ factors. The actual source and guard-table costs are retained explicitly.
\end{enumerate}
The construction is finite for each fixed $(n,T,K)$ and source. Its quantified dimension depends on these parameters.
\end{theorem}

The external input variables are not counted as existential witnesses. Canonical signs and initial strict order can instead be checked internally with the additional ledger below. For $T=0$, the externally promised canonical sorted domain admits a witness-free endpoint polynomial. Internal rejection of arbitrary unsorted tuples requires comparison auxiliaries, so that stronger interface is not witness-free. For $n=0,1$, every factor is the identity and a witness-free identity specialization is also available. The uniform scheduler works at these masses with zero candidate slots.

\textbf{What is implemented.} The package supplies a general fixed-slot scheduler reference and tests against the pinned factor evaluator. It separately supplies a complete coefficient emitter for all two-particle inputs of one small fixed source. The arbitrary-mass theorem is given by the explicit finite circuit construction in this report; there is no shipped arbitrary-mass coefficient exporter. The conservative macro bound is a theorem-level upper bound, not a measured general compiler count. The small sample's coefficient counts are mechanically emitted and checked.

\section{Source data and inherited local semantics}\label{fpa:eb:sec:source}
\subsection{Constants and costs}
Write $m$ for the number of controls, $p$ for moving branches, $a$ for direct branches, and $b=p+a$. The source has a nonnegative guard cutoff $J$. The unchanged constants are
\begin{align*}
D&=2m+4p,& S_0&=2D+2,&L_0&=3D+4,\\
B_3&=4D+5,&Z&=10B_3+10+2J,&F&=8pD+29p+m+a.
\end{align*}
Let $\Delta$ be the maximum size of a signed home-mode incidence list. Its outgoing entries are dispatch/direct factors and its incoming entries are commit/direct factors. There are exactly $2b$ actual incidence records in total. Set
\[
c=(J+2)^2,\qquad \tabsize=m+b+2bc+1.
\]
The $2bc$ term counts the actual domain and image guard-table entries. Source names, guard syntax, and their byte lengths are separate setup data. No $F$-entry table, packed $F$-bit truth table, or cost-free giant coefficient is admitted.

\subsection{What one factor means}
A local-factor descriptor contains two equal-size endpoint shapes of size two or three; a local isolation parameter $B$; the isolation radius $3B+1$; a competing-key radius
\[
\max(4B+1,T_{\rm context}+B)+1;
\]
and, when contextual, the branch and domain/image guard identifier. Here $T_{\rm context}$ is a descriptor radius, unrelated to the CA horizon $T$. Endpoint shapes are not translates of each other. A raw key identifies a translated endpoint that occurs with the exact local particle count. An active key additionally passes isolation, the all-raw-key competitor test, and its context guard.

The inherited isolated-swap theorem says that active endpoint unions are disjoint and the factor simultaneously swaps all active endpoints. A factor is never a sequential scan that rewrites one occurrence and then decides another on the modified configuration. The complete descriptor definitions, arithmetic indexing proof, and all-finite-support completeness lemma are included as the bundled Report 19 proof \cite{fpa:r19}. They are mathematical dependencies, not assumptions about well-formed encoded inputs.

\subsection{Arithmetic factor indices}
For clarity, here are the fixed formulas used by the schema. Put
\[
r=D+3,\quad U_E=2D+6,\quad A_E=4D+15,
\quad V_P=2D+7,\quad C_P=4D+14,
\]
and $E_0=pA_E+a$. A moving branch index is $e\in\{0,\ldots,p-1\}$; $k=0,1$ denotes outbound/inbound. Indices are zero based.
\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}lll@{}}\toprule
Factor & Index & Parameter range\\\midrule
$E$ free & $eA_E+kU_E$ & \\
$E$ behind & $eA_E+kU_E+1+t-S_0$ & $S_0\leq t\leq L_0$\\
$E$ ahead & $eA_E+kU_E+r+t-S_0$ & $S_0+1\leq t\leq L_0$\\
Dispatch & $eA_E+4D+12$ & \\
Endpoint & $eA_E+4D+13$ & \\
Commit & $eA_E+4D+14$ & \\
Direct branch $j$ & $pA_E+j$ & $0\leq j<a$\\\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The $P$ factors follow the $E$ block. Home phase for control $q$ has index $E_0+pC_P+q$. For moving branch $e$, the free phase has index $E_0+eC_P+kV_P$. The two near-phase families have indices
\begin{align*}
\text{negative side:}&\quad E_0+eC_P+kV_P+1+t-S_0,\\
\text{positive side:}&\quad E_0+eC_P+kV_P+D+4+t-S_0,
\end{align*}
where $S_0\leq t\leq L_0$ and the side is the sign of $H=h-z$. These formulas invert into a descriptor using comparisons, fixed-divisor quotient/remainder, and source-record selection. They do not require enumerating $F$ factors.

\section{Canonical quadratic primitive compiler}\label{fpa:eb:sec:primitive}
Every internal signed wire $v$ is represented by two natural variables $v^+,v^-$. An arithmetic assignment $v=f$ emits
\begin{equation}\label{fpa:eb:eq:assign}
v^+-v^--f=0,\qquad v^+v^-=0.
\end{equation}
For any fixed preceding values there is exactly one natural solution: the positive and negative parts of $f$. Allowed assignments are binary addition, subtraction, multiplication, fixed-integer scaling, and selection
\[
f=u+e(w-u),
\]
where $e$ is a previously determined Boolean wire. Long sums become binary folds. Products of already quadratic expressions are materialized first; all defining residuals have degree at most two.

\begin{lemma}[Unique comparison and constant division]\label{fpa:eb:lem:primitives}
For fixed signed integers $x,y$, natural $\beta,d$ satisfying
\begin{equation}\label{fpa:eb:eq:comparison}
\beta(\beta-1)=0,\qquad y-x-(2\beta-1)d-\beta+1=0
\end{equation}
are unique. They are $(1,y-x)$ if $y\geq x$ and $(0,x-y-1)$ otherwise. For a fixed positive integer $d_0$, natural $q^+,q^-,r,h$ satisfying
\begin{equation}\label{fpa:eb:eq:division}
q^+q^-=0,\quad v-d_0(q^+-q^-)-r=0,\quad r+h-(d_0-1)=0
\end{equation}
are unique and give the Euclidean quotient and remainder of $v$ by $d_0$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The first residual in \eqref{fpa:eb:eq:comparison} restricts $\beta$ to $0$ or $1$. In each case the second fixes $d$, and naturality admits precisely the indicated case. In \eqref{fpa:eb:eq:division}, naturality gives $0\leq r<d_0$. Euclidean quotient and remainder are unique even when $v$ is negative; sign separation and the last equation then uniquely fix all four variables.
\end{proof}
\wtag\ \textbf{Credit and a second presentation.} The circuit-to-quartic layer of this section and of Section~\ref{fpa:eb:sec:proof} is already printed in the collection. Signed natural pairs~\eqref{fpa:eb:eq:assign} are Lemma \path{cdc:pt:lem:signed}, and the topological induction with the sum of squares is the canonical circuit lemma \path{cdc:pt:lem:circuit}, of \path{canonical-diophantine-certificates} (CDC); the comparison gadget~\eqref{fpa:eb:eq:comparison} is CDC's Lemma \path{cdc:mem:lem:compare}, printed also in SMC Part~IV as \path{smc:sl:eq:cmp}; equality as a difference of two comparison bits is SMC's \path{smc:sl:eq:eq}; the constant division~\eqref{fpa:eb:eq:division} is the congruence gadget of SMC's Section \path{smc:sl:sec:presburger-quartic}. Report~20 cites Report~8 (SMC source~13) for these gadgets and proves them again; it does not cite CDC. The section is kept as Report~20's own presentation; neither Report~20 nor this report claims novelty for it.

Equality is built from the flags of $y\geq x$ and $y\geq x+1$, with their difference materialized as a Boolean output. The $x+1$ assignment is charged. Ties have a prescribed result. Absolute value is a comparison plus signed selection; order, minimum, floor, and remainder are never implicit primitives.

Boolean AND, OR, NOT, and affine copy use one natural output $z$ and respectively the residual
\[
z-uv,\qquad z-u-v+uv,\qquad z-1+u,
\]
or the appropriate affine equation. Their inputs are already forced Boolean wires, so Booleanity and uniqueness follow inductively. All large conjunctions and disjunctions are binary trees or folds.

\subsection{Exact primitive ledger}
Let $A,I,\Dv,G$ be the numbers of arithmetic assignments, comparisons, constant divisions, and Boolean gates. Let $Q$ count acceptance and external-admissibility residuals. Then
\begin{align}
V&=2A+2I+4\Dv+G,\label{fpa:eb:eq:V}\\
E&=2A+2I+3\Dv+G+Q.\label{fpa:eb:eq:E}
\end{align}
These are exact compiler ledgers, before any optional elimination of wires or residuals. The following coefficient-list bounds retain zero or cancelling monomial slots if necessary:
\begin{align*}
L_1&\leq9A+10I+9\Dv+4G+4Q,\\
L_2&\leq65A+68I+35\Dv+16G+16Q.
\end{align*}
Here $L_1$ is the number of residual monomial slots and $L_2$ is the number of ordered term occurrences in all expanded squares. Assignment row lengths are at most $8,1$; comparison row lengths at most $2,8$; division row lengths at most $1,5,3$; Boolean rows and final checks at most four. Squaring each row gives these stated bounds. Collected expanded monomials may be fewer, but their collection is a separately charged operation.

\textbf{False masks do not erase wires.} Every internal gate below a false mask is still evaluated and constrained. An exposed inactive candidate index is $F$; unused shape positions are zero; idle states retain their previous values. Internal dummy wires have unique canonical values, which need not be zero. Merely multiplying a defining equation by an enable bit would leave hidden freedom and would not prove this theorem.

\section{Fixed candidate slots without a factor array}\label{fpa:eb:sec:candidates}
For every occupied pair $x_i<x_j$, let $d=x_j-x_i$. Allocate the following ordered slots:
\begin{enumerate}
\item one home-phase slot;
\item $\Delta$ home-incidence slots in the pinned incidence order;
\item one moving free-$E$ slot and one moving free-$P$ slot;
\item for every $k\notin\{i,j\}$, four slots: behind, ahead, phase-near, and interaction.
\end{enumerate}
Thus there are exactly
\begin{equation}\label{fpa:eb:eq:C}
C=\binom n2\bigl[\Delta+3+4\max(n-2,0)\bigr]
\end{equation}
slots. A slot consists of an enable bit and an index; disabled slots carry index $F$. Duplicates are retained, with fixed ranks. Only the enabled set, not an arbitrary ordering of that set, has to match the candidate set of Report 19.

For a home gap $1\leq d\leq2m$, decode
\[
q=\left\lfloor\frac{d-1}{2}\right\rfloor,
\]
with odd/even gap giving plus/minus. A home phase or incidence slot is enabled only if $x_i-S_0$ is occupied. The incidence records are ordered by the source-order-preserving moving-branch filter followed by the source-order-preserving direct-branch filter. This need not agree with the original interleaved branch order.

For $2m<d\leq D$, quotient/remainder of $d-2m-1$ by $4$ gives the moving branch, outbound/inbound, and sign. Put $h=x_i$, $z=x_k$, $H=h-z$, and $\epsilon=0$ for odd $d$ and $1$ for even $d$. With branch side $s$, set $w=s$ outbound and $w=-s$ inbound. Both free slots are enabled. The triple slots test
\begin{align*}
\text{behind:}&\quad S_0\leq wH-\epsilon\leq L_0,\\
\text{ahead:}&\quad S_0+1\leq-wH+\epsilon\leq L_0,\\
\text{phase near:}&\quad S_0\leq |H|\leq L_0.
\end{align*}
The interaction slot uses four mutually exclusive cases:
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lll@{}}\toprule
Mode & Additional equality & Factor\\\midrule
Outbound plus & $H=-sS_0$ & Endpoint\\
Outbound minus & $H=sS_0$ & Dispatch\\
Inbound plus & $H=sS_0$ & Commit\\
Inbound minus & $H=-sS_0$ & Endpoint\\\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
All factor indices use the formulas above. A disabled mode is masked to a valid dummy before division, record lookup, or range-dependent arithmetic. If $p=0$, use a constant dummy moving record with side $+1$ and false enable, rather than selecting a nonexistent record. A disabled travel parameter is masked to $S_0$.

\subsection{Paying for source selection}
A table of size $m\Delta$ is unnecessary and not hidden in the cost. Each of the $2b$ actual incidence records stores its control, sign, rank, and factor index. Compare the selected control/sign with each actual record and route its masked contribution to its fixed rank accumulator. Each rank contains its unique match or the sentinel. Comparisons, masks, and accumulator additions are all circuit operations. Moving-record attributes use a linear-size multiplexer over the actual $p$ records.

\begin{lemma}[Candidate completeness]\label{fpa:eb:lem:candidates}
Every nonidentity factor on the current support appears in at least one enabled slot. A factor outside the enabled set has no raw occurrence.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Each two-particle endpoint has an occupied close pair. Every triple endpoint has exactly one close pair; the third particle is one of the enumerated $x_k$. The gap decoder and the displayed finite cases are exactly those of the pinned all-finite-support candidate lemma. Hence each raw occurrence yields a slot for its factor. Isolation, guards, and well-formed encoding are not used in this enumeration, so malformed supports are included. Repeated discoveries merely yield duplicate slots.
\end{proof}

\section{Exact simultaneous evaluation of each candidate}\label{fpa:eb:sec:factor}
The scheduler evaluates the entire effect of every candidate from the same current snapshot. A disabled slot selects factor zero as a total dummy and masks its final effect. A valid source has $F\geq1$. The descriptor is decoded arithmetically, with source-linear multiplexers. It contains two endpoint shapes of equal size $s\in\{2,3\}$ in fixed three-position arrays. Sort by presence first and coordinate second, so the unused zero record is last even if actual shape coordinates are negative. A fixed three-record compare-exchange network suffices.

\subsection{Raw keys and isolation}
For label $\ell\in\{0,1\}$ and each occupied coordinate $x_i$, form
\[
u_{\ell,i}=x_i-\operatorname{shape}_{\ell}[0].
\]
There are exactly $2n$ key slots. The raw flag requires every necessary translated shape site to be occupied, using equality against all $n$ coordinates. It also requires exactly $s$ particles in the inclusive interval $[u-B,u+B]$, using two comparisons per particle and a binary count sum.

Within one label, distinct $x_i$ yield distinct keys. Across labels, the nontranslation property prevents two raw labels at one key. Thus raw keys have no duplicate valid representations; no existential choice of occurrence is present.

Isolation uses the same inclusive counting circuit on $[u-(3B+1),u+(3B+1)]$. Every key slot is compared against all $2n$ raw slots and is rejected when a \emph{different raw key} lies within the competing-key radius. Competitors include raw keys that fail their own isolation or context. Discarding such failed competitors would change the factor and is unsound.

\subsection{Finite context guards}
For each contextual side, count occupied sites in
\[
[u-Z-J,u-Z]\qquad\text{or}\qquad[u+Z,u+Z+J].
\]
A count greater than one rejects the guard. Zero gives the overflow class $J+1$. One gives the exact offset of its unique particle, obtained by a stable first-hit scan with Boolean selections. On a rejected multiple-particle context, select a valid dummy class before table lookup.

The lookup compares the two class fields and the branch/domain-image identifier \emph{fieldwise} with all actual $2bc$ table entries, and forms the uniquely selected output by a finite masked sum. A flattened address such as $c_0(J+2)+c_1$ is deliberately not formed: it would introduce an unnecessary $J^2$-scale wire. If $b=0$, the unused lookup has a fixed true dummy output and accesses no nonexistent row. No guard routine scans a physical interval of $J+1$ coordinates.

\subsection{The simultaneous output and canonical order}
All active flags are decided from the unchanged pre-factor snapshot. Pair the sorted old endpoint sites with the sorted new endpoint sites by rank. For each old particle $x_j$, construct
\begin{equation}\label{fpa:eb:eq:simultaneous}
y_j=x_j+\sum_{a,r}
\bigl[\operatorname{active}_a\wedge x_j=u_a+\operatorname{old}_{a,r}\bigr]
\bigl(\operatorname{new}_{a,r}-\operatorname{old}_{a,r}\bigr).
\end{equation}
The rank $r$ ranges over live shape positions; the padded third position has zero displacement. The sum has at most $6n$ terms for each $j$, expanded into binary additions and lifted products. By the isolated-swap lemma, active endpoint unions are disjoint, so at most one displacement applies to any old particle. Formula \eqref{fpa:eb:eq:simultaneous} is exactly the simultaneous factor output, without particle loss, ambiguous labels, or a serial-rewrite artifact.

Sort the $n$ output coordinates with a fixed adjacent compare-exchange network using $\binom n2$ comparators, keeping the earlier record on ties. Factor semantics ensure distinct output coordinates. The sorted output is therefore canonical. Compare it coordinatewise with the input to obtain the changing bit. This comparison avoids needing to assume that a nonempty active list must change the set, even though that implication is available from the swap construction.

\section{Least future change and the completion rounds}\label{fpa:eb:sec:scheduler}
For $T>0$, set $R=T+K$. The state at round $r$ is a sorted support $X_r$, a step counter $t_r$, and a cursor $c_r$ denoting the least unprocessed factor index. Initially $t_0=c_0=0$. In every round, build all $C$ slots and all their factor outputs from $X_r$.

Scan the slots in their fixed order. Start with best index $F$ and retain a candidate precisely when
\[
\operatorname{enabled},\quad i\geq c_r,\quad g_i(X_r)\neq X_r,
\quad t_r<T,
\]
and its index is strictly smaller than the current best. The stored output is selected coordinatewise. Strict replacement forces the earliest slot among duplicated minimum indices. Comparisons and selections explicitly implement this minimum; there is no minimum oracle.

There are three mutually exclusive updates.
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{A winner exists.} Apply its already computed output; set $c_{r+1}=i+1$ and keep $t_{r+1}=t_r$. Recompute the candidate list from the new snapshot next round.
\item \textbf{The step is unfinished and no winner exists.} Keep the support, set $t_{r+1}=t_r+1$, and reset $c_{r+1}=0$.
\item \textbf{The horizon is complete.} Keep the support and $t=T$, with cursor zero. Every eligibility bit is false; all internal computations still have uniquely forced values.
\end{enumerate}
The final acceptance residual requires $t_R=T$, along with any endpoint equations.

\begin{lemma}[Absence certifies the remaining ordered suffix]\label{fpa:eb:lem:absence}
In the second case, every remaining factor is the identity on the current snapshot, so the update completes exactly one literal CA step.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Every future candidate was evaluated on that snapshot and failed to change it. By Lemma~\ref{fpa:eb:lem:candidates}, every noncandidate factor has no raw occurrence. Each factor in the remaining ordered suffix is therefore the identity. Applying these identities in their literal order leaves the same snapshot for the next factor, so the full suffix is the identity. Testing only a proposed winning event, without these absence decisions, would not establish this fact.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Exact total budget]\label{fpa:eb:lem:budget}
An execution with $k$ changing factors over $T$ complete steps uses exactly $k+T$ nonidle rounds, followed by uniquely forced idle padding. It reaches $t_R=T$ by $R=T+K$ if and only if $k\leq K$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Induct on the literal ordered factor sequence. In a changing round, the minimum future changing candidate is the next nonidentity factor: preceding factors are identities, so they preserve the snapshot on which that choice was tested. After a change, recomputation discovers any newly created later candidates while never revisiting passed indices. By Lemma~\ref{fpa:eb:lem:absence}, a no-winner round completes exactly the remaining suffix of one step. There is one such round per step, including the step containing the last allowed change. Thus there are exactly $k$ changing rounds and $T$ completion rounds. All later rounds are idle, proving the equivalence.
\end{proof}

The $+T$ is indispensable: $K$ event slots alone cannot pay for the final absence check of every step. Conversely, duplicating an event or choosing a later eligible factor cannot manufacture a new witness, because the stable minimum circuit fixes the schedule.

\section{Proof of the complete natural fiber statement}\label{fpa:eb:sec:proof}
For a fixed admissible input, evaluate the straight-line circuit in its declared order. At each arithmetic assignment, equation \eqref{fpa:eb:eq:assign} has one natural solution. At each comparison or constant division, Lemma~\ref{fpa:eb:lem:primitives} gives one natural extension. Boolean gates have uniquely determined outputs, inductively Boolean. Finite multiplexer sums, sorting networks, minimum scans, and scheduler updates are compositions of these primitives. All masked internal gates remain constrained and totalized.

It follows by induction over \emph{every primitive gate}, not merely over the displayed state snapshots, that the pre-acceptance system has exactly one complete natural assignment. This includes all inactive candidate descriptors, guard dummies, quotient/remainder slacks, losing outputs, and idle-round computations. The acceptance residuals either retain that assignment or remove it. Lemma~\ref{fpa:eb:lem:budget} identifies completion with $k_T(X)\leq K$, and the target equations identify its output with the prescribed endpoint.

Finally, over natural numbers (indeed over integers), a sum of integer squares is zero exactly when every residual is zero. The sum-of-squares polynomial has degree at most four and exactly the same natural fiber as the residual system. This proves Theorem~\ref{fpa:eb:thm:main}.

\wtag\ The induction above is CDC's canonical circuit lemma \path{cdc:pt:lem:circuit} (note after Lemma~\ref{fpa:eb:lem:primitives}). The closest theorem in the collection is SMC Part~IV's Theorem \path{smc:sl:thm:core}, a sparse finite-horizon quartic with exactly one natural zero for number-conserving partitioned automata. Theorem~\ref{fpa:eb:thm:main} is a statement of that kind for a different automaton, the frozen ordered rule; its new ingredients are the event budget $K$, the $T$ separate absence-and-completion rounds and the occupied-pair candidate slots, which make the circuit scale with $(T+K)C$ rather than with $T$ times the factor count $F$.

The argument is specific to natural auxiliaries. The comparison slack, sign separation, and nonnegativity conditions are not a claim of unique or equivalent real solutions. Finite one-coordinate witness mutation tests support the implementation, but they do not replace this deterministic-induction proof of complete uniqueness.

\section{Fully charged size and expansion bounds}\label{fpa:eb:sec:cost}
For $T>0$, define
\begin{equation}\label{fpa:eb:eq:U}
U=10000(T+K)(C+1)(n+1)^2\tabsize.
\end{equation}
The binary-fanin construction can be chosen so that
\[
A+I+\Dv+G\leq U.
\]
This is intentionally generous, with all source multiplexer and table costs retained. The per-round macro allocation below makes its scale explicit.
\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}p{0.57\linewidth}p{0.34\linewidth}@{}}\toprule
Work & Primitive upper allocation\\\midrule
Candidates, incidence selection, and all index/descriptor decoders & $1000(C+1)(n+1)\tabsize$\\
All candidate effects, raw and competitor tests, contexts, table selection, displacement, sorting & $1000(C+1)(n+1)^2\tabsize$\\
Stable minimum, completion and idle flags, state updates & $1000(C+1)(n+1)\tabsize$\\\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
These sum to at most $3000(C+1)(n+1)^2\tabsize$, leaving slack within \eqref{fpa:eb:eq:U} for initialization, finite descriptor cases, and final wiring. Each lookup row needs at most eight comparisons, a fixed AND tree, a mask, and an accumulator addition. A required-site test uses two comparisons and a Boolean fold. A two-ended interval test uses two comparisons, one AND, and one addition. A compare-exchange uses a comparison and two signed selections. Every loop is over a fixed set of size $n,2n,3,\Delta,m,p,b$, or $2bc$. No loop is over $F$ or coordinate extent. This allocation is not a claim that a macro has exactly its displayed number of gates.

\subsection{Independent per macro construction check}
The independent audit gives the following more concrete accounting, in the primitive units of Section~\ref{fpa:eb:sec:primitive}. Equality costs at most four primitives; interval membership three; interval membership with a running count four; absolute value three; and an ordinary signed compare-exchange three. A fieldwise guard row needs three equalities, two conjunctions, a constant-bit mask and an accumulator fold, at most sixteen primitives.

For one of a descriptor's $2n$ raw slots, the audit allocates: one primitive for the key; $15n+12$ for three presence scans and their masks; $4n+7$ for raw-window count and size equality; $4n+6$ for isolation; $20n$ for all competing raw slots; $22n+19$ for the two context scans and their totalization; $32bc+3$ for all guard rows and noncontextual bypass; and five for active/masking logic. These total at most $65n+53+32bc$. Across $2n$ raw slots, then allowing $60n^2$ for all displacements and $2n^2+7n$ for outputs, sorting and the changing flag, one candidate-effect bound is
\[
200n^2+120n+64nbc+20.
\]
Multiplication by $C$ remains below its $1000(C+1)(n+1)^2\tabsize$ macro allocation. Retaining a separate branch field instead of a combined guard identifier replaces $64nbc$ by $84nbc$, still within the allocation.

Writing $P_2=\binom n2$, a deliberately wasteful source/candidate/descriptor allowance is
\[
P_2(200n+200b+100\Delta+1000)+C(2000+200\tabsize).
\]
The second term permits up to 120 primitives for each of ten finite descriptor families, 200 for the two three-position sorters, 100 for partitions, 100 for fixed wiring, and $200\tabsize$ for record selection. For $n\geq2$, the inequalities $C\geq3P_2$ and $C\geq\Delta P_2$ place this allowance below $1000(C+1)(n+1)\tabsize$. If $n<2$, these loops are empty. The stable minimum and state update need at most $C(n+12)+10n+40$ primitives, well inside their separate macro allocation. Initialization is $O(n)$ and final checks are in $Q$. These explicit loop charges support the conservative 10000 prefactor without pretending to have mechanically measured a general exporter.

Equations \eqref{fpa:eb:eq:V}--\eqref{fpa:eb:eq:E} imply
\begin{align*}
V&\leq4U,& E&\leq3U+Q,\\
L_1&\leq10U+4Q,&L_2&\leq68U+16Q.
\end{align*}
At most $Q\leq3n+1$ additional checks suffice for $n$ external sign constraints, $n-1$ strict-order checks, $n$ target equations, and one completion equation. Their comparison gates are included in $U$. On an externally promised sorted canonical input domain, omit the admissibility checks. The $T=0$ specialization is accounted separately.

\subsection{Source preprocessing is separate}
The pinned validator visits all $bc$ branch/class pairs, evaluates guard syntax, and constructs the full domain and image tables plus incidence metadata. Retaining that implementation costs $O(cG_{\rm src}+bc)$ scalar guard-program operations and a coarse $O(bc^2)$ bit-work bound for growing masks, in addition to parsing and integer-comparison costs. Source syntax and $2bc$ table entries occupy actual storage. Large binary $J$ is not normalized cheaply; there is no polynomial-in-$\log J$ preprocessing claim.

\subsection{The universal-source count is only a slot count}
For the pinned universal source,
\[
m=122622,\quad b=141561,\quad J=0,\quad\Delta=2,
\quad F=269291358255.
\]
At $n=5$, formula \eqref{fpa:eb:eq:C} gives
\[
C=10(2+3+12)=170,\qquad \tabsize=1396672.
\]
This is \emph{170 candidate slots}, not 170 witnesses, equations, or operations. A generic per-use multiplexer still pays the full source and guard-table costs. The resulting crude $U$ is enormous; the theorem does not claim a practical universal quartic exporter. It removes the obligation to allocate one witness block for every factor position in every literal CA step, while preserving the costs actually used by this construction.

\section{Coordinate heights and bit complexity}\label{fpa:eb:sec:bits}
Let $H_0=\max_i|x_i|$, with $H_0=0$ for the empty input. On an accepting execution, each matched particle displacement in one changing factor is at most $2B_3$. Actual snapshots therefore have coordinate magnitude at most $H_0+2KB_3$; hypothetical candidate outputs need one additional such displacement. Keys, shape offsets, and context endpoints add $O(B_3+Z+J)$. Indices are at most $F$, time is at most $T$, and particle counts are at most $2n$.

For the specific totalized construction above, a coarse bound on the magnitude of every canonical auxiliary is
\begin{equation}\label{fpa:eb:eq:height}
\mathrm{Ht}_* =100(n+1)\bigl(H_0+2(K+2)B_3+Z+J+F+T+K+1\bigr).
\end{equation}
It covers comparison slacks, constant quotient/remainder, source codes, factor indices, class offsets, masked displacements, count accumulators, and canonical signed parts. Before dependent arithmetic, invalid modes and travel parameters are masked to fixed valid dummies. Guard lookup is fieldwise. A stable first-hit scan avoids accumulating many unbounded context offsets. Source-record selectors are one-hot, so partial selected-field sums are bounded by the largest selected field. Remaining multiplication is by a Boolean, sign, or the fixed factor-index constants; there is no hidden product of arbitrary coordinate magnitudes.

\wtag\ Report~20 writes this bound $H_*$. It is printed $\mathrm{Ht}_*$ here because Report~28 (Section~\ref{fpa:pq:sec:height}) uses $H_*$ for an isolation distance; nothing else changed.

Thus $W=\lceil\log_2(\mathrm{Ht}_*+1)\rceil$ bits per auxiliary suffice, and the complete witness uses at most $VW$ bits, beyond the external input encoding. This accepting-fiber bound is not a promise about adversarial submitted values. A rejected budget can still be evaluated using an analogous bound with $R$ in place of $K$.

Let $\lambda$ be the largest actual residual coefficient bit length. Trace-generation constants are at most a fixed multiple of $F+Z+J+T+1$. Prescribed target constants appear only in acceptance equations and are charged separately in $\lambda$. Symbolic coordinate inputs do not make coefficients depend on $H_0$; specializing the input coordinates to constants instead must charge their bit lengths in $\lambda$.

The residual coefficient list has $O(U+Q)$ terms. With schoolbook arithmetic, straight-line witness generation costs
\[
O\bigl((U+Q)W^2\bigr)
\]
elementary bit operations, with source-constant lengths included in $W$, plus preprocessing. Direct residual and sum-of-squares verification is bounded by
\[
O\bigl((U+Q)(W+\lambda+\log(U+Q+2))^2\bigr).
\]
For an arbitrary submitted tuple, use its actual maximum input/auxiliary bit length in place of $W$, or reject an oversized encoding after checking its length. This charges intermediate products, squaring, and accumulation rather than treating giant arithmetic as unit cost.

If every residual coefficient has magnitude at most $C_0$, each collected expanded coefficient has magnitude at most $L_2C_0^2$ and bit length at most
\[
2\lambda+\lceil\log_2(L_2+1)\rceil+1.
\]
The full expansion enumerates at most $L_2$ ordered term occurrences and collects matching degree-at-most-four monomials. Its output storage and coefficient accumulation are separate costs; no equality of factored and expanded representation sizes is assumed.

\section{Complete implemented mass two example}\label{fpa:eb:sec:example}
The source has controls \texttt{q, halt}, $J=0$, and one branch from \texttt{q} to \texttt{halt} with side $+1$, update $+1$, and true guard. It has $D=8$ and $F=95$. Triple endpoints cannot occur at mass two. The only potentially changing factors are the outbound/inbound free $E$ factors at indices $0,22$ and free $P$ factors at indices $47,70$. The exporter specializes those four exact factor actions directly; it is not a general descriptor compiler.

For this implementation only, the symbolic input uses three natural coordinates
\[
(h^+,h^-,g),\qquad h=h^+-h^-,\quad h^+h^-=0,
\quad X=(h,h+g+1).
\]
The gap-minus-one encoding enforces strict order without an input comparison. It differs from the general theorem's four-coordinate canonical-pair interface but represents every sorted mass-two support uniquely. All endpoint values are checked to be exact integers and strictly increasing before they become coefficients; Boolean and floating-point endpoints are rejected.

The normative sample uses $X=(-10,-5)$, $T=1$, $K=2$, and target $Y=(-9,-4)$. Its input values are $(0,10,4)$. The deterministic execution is
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}rllll@{}}\toprule
Round & Action & Support afterward & Time & Cursor\\\midrule
0 & Factor $0$ & $(-9,-3)$ & 0 & 1\\
1 & Factor $47$ & $(-9,-4)$ & 0 & 48\\
2 & Absence and completion & $(-9,-4)$ & 1 & 0\\\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The third round is necessary even though both permitted changes have already occurred. A budget $K=1$ rejects this input for one full CA step.

\begin{samepage}
The emitted primitive ledger and polynomial counts are
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lr@{}}\toprule
Quantity & Exact sample value\\\midrule
Arithmetic assignments $A$ & 189\\
Comparisons $I$ & 78\\
Constant divisions $\Dv$ & 0\\
Boolean gates $G$ & 78\\
Acceptance/admissibility residuals $Q$ & 4\\
Natural auxiliary variables $V$ & 612\\
Quadratic-or-lower residuals $E$ & 616\\
Residual monomials & 2013\\
Ordered expanded-square term occurrences & 9955\\
Collected expanded quartic monomials & 4573\\
Largest witness bit length & 7\\
Largest residual coefficient bit length & 7\\\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
\end{samepage}
The residuals include external sign separation, horizon completion, and two endpoint equations. Formula \eqref{fpa:eb:eq:V} gives $378+156+78=612$ and \eqref{fpa:eb:eq:E} gives $378+156+78+4=616$. These values describe this particular small source and sample, not the general schema or universal-source instantiation.

\subsection{A coefficient explicit artifact rather than an abbreviation}
The bundled \texttt{example-polynomial.json} contains every residual coefficient, every variable name in declared order, and the entire collected expanded quartic. A term is serialized as
\[
[\text{integer coefficient},\,[i_1,\ldots,i_d]],
\]
meaning the coefficient times $v_{i_1}\cdots v_{i_d}$; an empty index list denotes a constant. Indices are sorted, zero coefficients omitted, and repeated monomials collected. The first three variables are external inputs and the next 612 are the auxiliaries. The companion \texttt{example-witness.json} gives all 612 natural values. These complete machine-readable files, rather than a many-page typesetting of 4573 terms, are the normative coefficient and witness listing.

The binding verifier regenerates the normative small circuit and checks the source, inputs, $T,K$, target, arity, variable order, ledger, residual rows, expansion, round decoder, and output. It rejects malformed types and noncanonical encodings. It then evaluates the supplied witness exactly. This is binding verification using the producer's small compiler; the separate audit independently reconstructs and checks the expanded polynomial and its natural witness. Neither claims an independent arbitrary-mass exporter.

\section{Reproducibility and audit evidence}\label{fpa:eb:sec:replay}
The release contains the complete finite-schema proof, fixed-slot scheduler, mass-two emitter, coefficient and witness files, exact receipts, independent audit, pinned evaluator and compiler, the compressed universal source needed by the count check, and dependency proof references. Python replay uses only the standard library. It does not fetch packages or resolve optional private files.

\wtag\ The compressed universal source \path{reproducibility/source.json.gz} is not shipped in this directory, and neither are the bundled copies of Report~19's evaluator and Report~15's compiler (shipped once, as \path{code/19-lazy-eval-lazy_reversible.py} and \path{code/15-rev-five-compiler-reversible_binary.py}) nor the dependency references; the README says how to rebuild or retrieve them (``Reconstructing the excluded data'').

The general scheduler tests compare candidate sets and results with the pinned lazy/eager factor products on small sources. Cases include empty and singleton supports, simultaneous separated copies, the known four-event malformed cascade, exact and insufficient budgets, exact completion-round counts, canonical idle padding, and multistep total budgets. The mass-two suite compares emitted polynomials with actual CA iteration, including 100-digit signed coordinates, and checks the complete sample expansion and every one-coordinate $+1$ witness mutation. Guard checks cover actual finite tables and boundary conditions. These are finite executable evidence, not a substitute for the general theorem.

The independent audit implements the full $2n$-slot raw/absence/context/displacement algorithm separately and compares it with literal original factor application. Its seven sources include no branches, direct-only behavior, left decrement, guarded increment, mixed original branch order, $J=17$ context, and multiple incidence branches. It checks 942 configurations, 123222 factor comparisons, 468 scheduler cases, and 71 strict underbudget rejections. It exercises 485 multi-active factor instances, 20 competitor exclusions, and 14 multiple-particle context rejections. Arithmetic checks include 392 exhaustive signed-division fibers, 36 sample syntax/ledger cases, and 189 simultaneous positive/negative-part mutations. Final interface replays reject 13 malformed targets, five other malformed inputs, and ten malformed serialized/source cases; a valid 301-digit-coordinate trace passes.

The packaged replay runs the applicable suites in ordinary and optimized Python modes; no mathematical rejection or validation depends on \texttt{assert}. File binding and exact integer checks are explicit. Conservative resource and integer-encoding limits keep this a practical sample-release checker rather than an unrestricted service for adversarial polynomials. Finite tests corroborate the construction but do not prove universality or replace its mathematical argument.

\subsection{Pinned dependencies and provenance}
The evaluator's byte identity is
\sha{42e8aa65c05fcf373a03a02be51ebb89a4fdcb1f1070ad776ffe1e8e99049c61}
The original frozen compiler's byte identity is
\sha{f95a6028c9d3cc6f8b0b497cfc0cad68104da6d70e55205df04ad234e5d0c76f}
The package verifies these mandatory local dependencies before execution. Any portability edits to research drivers are recorded as original-versus-adapted hashes; the frozen rule implementation is not silently modified. Exact file inventories and byte lengths are checked, with extra-file and symlink rejection. A manifest is an integrity record tied to the release; it is not a cryptographic author identity or an external proof of authenticity.

The README gives the authoritative replay and build commands, expected outcomes, implementation limits, and manifest policy. An extracted copy is replayed independently of the authoring directories before delivery. LaTeX source is supplied for the report; rebuilding the PDF needs TeX Live, while numerical replay does not. Report 8's canonical comparison and signed-division constructions are included as local references \cite{fpa:eb:r8}; no MRDP existence theorem is invoked.

\section{Limits and the next concrete improvement}\label{fpa:rep:20:last}
A bare list of changing factors proves too little. It does not certify that an earlier omitted factor was an identity, that a remaining suffix has no change, or that all active occurrences of the selected factor were applied simultaneously. The present construction pays for those obligations by evaluating the whole fixed candidate circuit from each snapshot.

The cost is event-budget-sensitive but is not merely proportional to active keys or to the operational lazy evaluator's number of tested factors. There are $T+K$ complete candidate rounds, including losing candidates and ghost computations in padded rounds. To inherit a smaller data-dependent lazy-test count, one would need a different explicit sequential-search transcript and its own fully paid bound.

No unconditional small numerical $K$ bound follows for arbitrary malformed finite supports: cascading changes are possible. $F$ remains present through factor-index lengths and source-dependent coefficients, though not as an allocated factor array. The universal mass-five candidate count does not show universality for mass two or a practical universal polynomial exporter. There is no claim of fixed quantified arity for unbounded horizons, of a finite-fold representation of all recursively enumerable sets, of a real-witness equivalence, or of a new optimality or novelty result.

\wtag\ (3~October 2026.) This step is still open for the ordered rule. Report~28 (Part~\ref{fpa:part:six}) ships a general emitter with measured ledgers, but for the new parallel rule of Report~26, which differs from the ordered rule on malformed supports (Section~\ref{fpa:pq:sec:cascade}); it is not an emitter for Theorem~\ref{fpa:eb:thm:main}.

The next engineering step is a general arbitrary-mass coefficient emitter implementing every explicit macro, with an independent binding checker for those macros and measured rather than conservative counts. The present release establishes the finite canonical-witness theorem and provides a complete smaller instance that can already be expanded and checked exactly.

\fpapart{The parallel two-involution rule: replacement, sparse evaluation and quartic certificates (Reports 26, 27 and 28)}{fpa:part:six}

\wtag\ The three Reports of this Part concern one new rule. Report~26 replaces the ordered product of Report~15's compiler by two parallel full-shift involutions, $F_{\mathrm{new}}=P_\parallel\circ E_\parallel$, that agree with the old rule on every admissible encoded state and deliberately differ on malformed ones; Report~27 evaluates the new rule exactly on every finite support without building its endpoint family; Report~28 compiles it, at each fixed horizon, into a degree-four polynomial with a unique natural witness. The three Reports write the same map as $F_{\mathrm{new}}$, $\Phi$ and $\mathcal F$ respectively. The Hilbert's-tenth programme reviewed all three in \path{Computability/HilbertTenthProblem/Papers/research-wip/native-stream-queue/review_parallel_particle_reports.md} (commit \texttt{fb7e3cb47}): it read their main proofs, enumerated all 4,096 period-12 words of Report~26's lemma example, reconstructed Report~28's 12,595-term quartic from its 1,502 residuals, found no defect, and records that the horizon-dependent arity gives no fixed-arity, finite-fold or universal result.

\reportopening{26}{Two parallel conservative involutions}{A full shift replacement for the five particle compiler}{Research report 26}

\begin{reportabstract}{26}
We replace the ordered local-gate construction of a binary five-particle cellular automaton by two parallel involutions. A general prospective-isolation lemma gives a full-shift, number-conserving involution of radius at most $3(b+r)$ from finitely many endpoint rewrites of write radius $b$ and candidate-read radius $r$. The selection test preserves the complete set of raw type-anchor keys, and a read/support separation argument proves invariance of every eligibility status, including initially rejected keys. Applied to the literal five-particle templates, the two blocks agree with the previous construction on its entire admissible doubled micrograph. The encoding, exact clocks, reflections, halt observer, and clean targets are unchanged. An explicit sufficient composite radius is $180D+258+9J$, with symbolic universal-ledger value $91\,711\,698$. The resulting rule intentionally differs on malformed inputs. We include the boundary proof, finite executable evidence, precise attribution, and a portable sealed replay package. We claim neither radius optimality nor novelty of the two-involution architecture; no universal new-rule evaluation or truth table is produced.
\end{reportabstract}

\wtag\ Delivered as \path{Two_Parallel_Conservative_Involutions_Package.zip} (batch~82, archive~20; arrival \texttt{db37d18c8}), dated 3~October 2026; files placed by \texttt{7d2b1b245} with the prefix \path{26-parallel-involutions-}. Report~26 keeps the endpoint templates of Report~15's compiler (Part~\ref{fpa:part:one}) and replaces their ordered execution; its universal ledger is Report~19's (Part~\ref{fpa:part:four}). Lemma~\ref{fpa:pi:lem:parallel} is a lemma proved here, in the marker and conserved-landscape tradition that Section~\ref{fpa:pi:sec:prior} names; Report~26 disclaims priority for it, and so does this report. Report~27 restates it (Section~\ref{fpa:rep:27:first}ff.). Report~26's macro \verb|\N| (printing $\mathbb Z_{\ge0}$) is \verb|\Nge| here; nothing printed changed. The delivered table of contents is omitted.

\section{Main result and the object that changes}\label{fpa:rep:26:first}\label{fpa:pi:sec:main}
A configuration is a word in the full binary shift $\X$. Its particles are its sites with value one. A cellular automaton (CA) here is a translation-equivariant map whose output at each site depends on a uniformly bounded neighborhood. A CA is number-conserving if it preserves the number of particles on every finite-particle configuration. No assertion equating divergent infinite sums is intended.

The starting point is the finite guarded-swap compiler of Report 15, pinned in the accompanying package as \file{scientific/frozen_reversible_binary.py}. Its SHA-256 is
\begin{center}\small\texttt{f95a6028c9d3cc6f8b0b497cfc0cad68104da6d70e55205df04ad234e5d0c76f}.\end{center}
That compiler produces an ordered list of individually reversible gates. Its old edge and phase blocks are products of many such gates. They are matchings on admissible encodings, but need not be involutions on malformed configurations. We keep its finite endpoint-template data and replace its global selection and execution mechanism.

\begin{theorem}[Source-uniform replacement]\label{fpa:pi:thm:main}
For every finite source accepted by the pinned compiler, there is a binary CA
\[
 \Fnew=\parP\circ\parE
\]
with the following properties.
\begin{enumerate}
\item Each block is an involution on \emph{all} of $\X$, is translation-equivariant, and conserves ordinary finite particle number.
\item The new rule agrees step for step with $\Fold$ on the entire admissible doubled micrograph, including every natural-counter home, both signs of every strict intermediate node of every enabled edge, and all translations.
\item It has sufficient radius
\[
 R_{\mathrm{new}}=180D+258+9J,\qquad D=2m+4p,
\]
where $m$ is the number of source controls, $p$ the number of nonzero-update branches, and $J$ the finite counter-class cut.
\item Its inverse is $\parE\circ\parP$. It is time-symmetric, with either of the individually number-conserving binary involutions $\parE,\parP$ as a time reverser.
\end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

The proof occupies Sections~\ref{fpa:pi:sec:lemma}--\ref{fpa:pi:sec:preservation}; the radius calculation is in Section~\ref{fpa:pi:sec:resources}. Equality on admissible states is a simulation theorem, not equality on the full shift. Section~\ref{fpa:pi:sec:counterexamples} gives an explicit malformed input on which the old and new rules differ. Thus the displayed improvement concerns a \emph{new extension of the same encoded dynamics}. It is not a smaller-radius presentation of the previous full-shift automaton.

The statements about a universal source are inherited conditionally through the previously established source construction and its admissible encoding. This report proves the replacement uniformly for all accepted sources; it does not re-prove the attributed universal-source theorem, materialize a universal template array, or execute that array. Mathematical existence, finite examples, and practical rule evaluation are kept distinct throughout.

\section{Prospective isolation on the full shift}\label{fpa:pi:sec:lemma}
\subsection{Write supports and read windows}
For $a\ge0$ and $u\in\Z$, put $I_a(u)=[u-a,u+a]\cap\Z$. Fix integers $0\le b\le r$ and a finite type set $G$. Each type $g$ has its own write set
\[
 W_g\subseteq I_b(0)
\]
and two distinct binary words $p_g,q_g$ on $W_g$ of equal Hamming weight. Define $\tau_{g,u}$ everywhere on $\X$: exchange $p_g$ and $q_g$ on $u+W_g$, leave every other word on that write set fixed, and never change coordinates outside $u+W_g$. Then $\tau_{g,u}^2=\id$.

The common bound $b$ is \emph{not} a requirement to erase or constrain every bit of $I_b(u)$. Bits in $I_b(u)\setminus(u+W_g)$ are retained. This distinction matters below because a free pair has a smaller exactness window than the common triple write bound.

Let $c_g(x,u)$ be a translation-covariant Boolean predicate depending only on $x|_{I_r(u)}$. Assume that candidacy implies that the word on $u+W_g$ is $p_g$ or $q_g$, and that the predicate is endpoint-symmetric:
\begin{equation}\label{fpa:pi:eq:symmetry}
 c_g(x,u)=c_g(\tau_{g,u}x,u).
\end{equation}
Extra empty-padding tests or contextual guards are permitted, provided all their reads are included in $r$. A \emph{raw key} is $(g,u)$; define
\[
 C(x)=\{(g,u):c_g(x,u)=1\}.
\]
Different types at the same anchor are different keys. Endpoint orientation is not part of the key.

\subsection{Selection without a recursive eligibility test}
Set $H=2(b+r)$. A raw key $k=(g,u)$ is selected precisely when
\begin{align}
 &k\in C(x),\label{fpa:pi:eq:candidate}\\
 &\text{there is no }(h,v)\in C(x)\setminus\{k\}\text{ with }|v-u|\le H,\label{fpa:pi:eq:isolation}\\
 &C(\tau_kx)\cap\bigl(G\times I_{b+r}(u)\bigr)
   =C(x)\cap\bigl(G\times I_{b+r}(u)\bigr).\label{fpa:pi:eq:prospective}
\end{align}
The last test compares \emph{all raw types}, before and after this one hypothetical rewrite. It does not compare only the current type, only the currently selected keys, or oriented endpoint labels. It does not recursively ask whether other keys are eligible.

Write $A(x)$ for the selected-key set, and let $T(x)$ apply the selected rewrites simultaneously. Distinct selected anchors have distance greater than $H\ge2b$, so their containing write intervals are disjoint. Consequently this definition makes sense even on an arbitrary bi-infinite dense input: an output site participates in at most one selected rewrite.

\begin{lemma}[Prospective-isolation lemma]\label{fpa:pi:lem:parallel}
For every $x\in\X$,
\[
 C(Tx)=C(x),\qquad A(Tx)=A(x),\qquad T^2x=x.
\]
The map $T$ is a translation-equivariant, number-conserving CA of radius at most $3(b+r)$. The type count does not enter this radius bound.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
We give all eligibility-invariance steps, since preservation of already selected keys alone would not prove involutivity.

\emph{A finite prospective check is global raw-key preservation.} A rewrite at $u$ can affect a raw predicate at $v$ only if its write set meets $I_r(v)$. Necessarily $|v-u|\le b+r$. Thus \eqref{fpa:pi:eq:prospective} is equivalent to
\begin{equation}\label{fpa:pi:eq:globalraw}
 C(\tau_kx)=C(x).
\end{equation}
This equivalence concerns raw predicates, not eligibility.

\emph{No cooperative births occur.} If one raw read window $I_r(v)$ met the supports of two selected rewrites at $u,w$, then
\[
 |u-w|\le |u-v|+|v-w|\le2(b+r)=H,
\]
contrary to isolation. Every raw predicate after the simultaneous update therefore sees either its original word, or exactly the word obtained after one selected rewrite alone. Equation~\eqref{fpa:pi:eq:globalraw} preserves its value in either case. This proves $C(Tx)=C(x)$ for every raw key, including keys initially absent.

\emph{The entire prospective read neighborhood is controlled.} Write $P_k(x)$ for the equality in \eqref{fpa:pi:eq:prospective}. Candidate anchors examined there lie within $b+r$ of $u$; each candidate reads radius $r$. The whole test is determined by
\begin{equation}\label{fpa:pi:eq:prospectivewindow}
 x|_{I_{b+2r}(u)}.
\end{equation}
The hypothetical rewrite needs no coordinates outside this interval. Since $\tau_k^2=\id$, interchanging the two sides of the comparison gives
\begin{equation}\label{fpa:pi:eq:owninvariance}
 P_k(\tau_kx)=P_k(x).
\end{equation}

\emph{Every eligibility status is invariant.} Raw-key invariance already preserves isolation. Consider any isolated raw key $k=(g,u)$, whether selected or not. Every other selected key $(h,v)$ is a different raw key, so $|v-u|>2b+2r$. Its write interval is disjoint from \eqref{fpa:pi:eq:prospectivewindow}. If $k$ was rejected, its full prospective-test neighborhood is therefore unchanged. If $k$ was selected, only its own rewrite affects that neighborhood, and \eqref{fpa:pi:eq:owninvariance} preserves the test. Nonisolated raw keys remain nonisolated; absent keys remain absent. This proves $A(Tx)=A(x)$, including the initially ineligible cases.

\emph{The second update is the inverse.} The second application uses exactly the same disjoint type-anchor rewrites. Each is an involution, so every changed block returns to its original word and every other site remains fixed.

\emph{Locality and conservation.} At anchor $u$, candidacy reads radius $r$, isolation reads radius $H+r$, and prospectivity reads radius $b+2r$. Their maximum is $2b+3r$. Only keys within distance $b$ can write a given output coordinate. Hence
\[
 R_T\le b+\max\{r,H+r,b+2r\}=3(b+r).
\]
There are finitely many types and finitely many anchors in every query, so this is a finite Boolean local rule. Translation covariance gives shift equivariance. Disjoint equal-weight rewrites conserve particle number on every finite input.
\end{proof}

For infinite inputs the same proof gives a well-defined local bijection. One may match input and output particles within each changed finite block to obtain bounded-displacement local transport; finite total mass is not required for the involution proof. The independent full-shift audit is frozen as \file{scientific/audit-lemma.md}.

\wtag\ The audit is shipped as \path{26-parallel-involutions-audit-lemma.md}. The hypothesis $b\le r$ of Section~\ref{fpa:pi:sec:lemma} is not used in the proof (observed at placement; Report~27 states the lemma with $r\ge b$, Report~28 without the order). The Hilbert's-tenth review \path{review_parallel_particle_reports.md} (\texttt{fb7e3cb47}) re-derives the lemma and the radius $3(b+r)$, and checks the period-12 example of Section~\ref{fpa:pi:sec:counterexamples} on all 4,096 words.

\section{The finite source and five particle geometry}\label{fpa:pi:sec:source}
\subsection{Source interface and admissibility}
An accepted source is a finite partial-injective deterministic machine on controls $Q$ and counters $(c_0,c_1)\in\Nge^2$. A branch $e:q\to q'$ selects the left or right counter, represented by $v\in\{-1,+1\}$, and changes it by $\Delta\in\{-1,0,+1\}$. Its Boolean guard $G_e(c)$ uses finitely many tests $c_j=k$ or $c_j>k$. Enabled updates must preserve natural counters. Domains of branches from each control are disjoint, and \emph{actual images} of branches entering each control are disjoint. The designated halt control has no outgoing branch.

For selected counter index $j$, the exact image predicate is
\[
 I_e(c')=G_e(c'-\Delta\mathbf e_j)
 \ \wedge\ (c'-\Delta\mathbf e_j\in\Nge^2).
\]
The cut $J$ ensures that all domains and images are determined by the two counter classes $0,1,\ldots,J,>J$. The pinned parser validates this finite interface, including unreachable rows; it does not accept a caller-supplied unchecked image predicate. Its complete schema is supplied as an inert reference.

There are $m=|Q|$ home modes $H_q$ and two moving modes $O_e,I_e$ for each of the $p$ nonzero branches. Give every signed mode a distinct gap $d(u^+),d(u^-)\in\{1,\ldots,D\}$, where
\begin{equation}\label{fpa:pi:eq:constants}
\begin{gathered}
 D=2m+4p,\quad S=2D+2,\quad B_2=D+1,\quad L=3D+4,\\
 b=B_3=4D+5=L+D+1,\quad K=3b+1,\quad Z=10b+10+2J.
\end{gathered}
\end{equation}
A head at anchor $x$ is the pair $\{x,x+d(u^{\pm})\}$. Its anchor is always the leftmost occupied site, including during leftward motion. With origin zero, a home encoding is
\begin{equation}\label{fpa:pi:eq:encoding}
 \operatorname{Enc}(q,c_0,c_1)^{\pm}
 =\{-(Z+c_0),0,Z+c_1,S,S+d(H_q^{\pm})\}.
\end{equation}
There are exactly five particles, no extra track, no spatial parity background, no clock particle, and no mass-free phase symbol.

\subsection{The unsigned micrograph}
A zero-update source edge is one home-to-home microedge. A nonzero enabled edge is subdivided as follows, with origin $o$, selected old marker $m$, and new marker $m'=m+v\Delta$:
\begin{enumerate}
\item Dispatch $H_q$ at $o+S$ to $O_e$ at $o+vS$, guarded by $G_e$.
\item Move the outbound anchor by $v$ until it reaches $m-vS$.
\item Move the selected marker from $m$ to $m'$ and change the head to $I_e$ at $m'-vS$.
\item Move the inbound anchor by $-v$ until it reaches $o+vS$.
\item Commit to $H_{q'}$ at $o+S$, guarded by $I_e$ on the post counters.
\end{enumerate}
The admissible unsigned graph contains every home ID over natural counters and only the strict intermediate configurations of these subdivisions of \emph{enabled} source edges. Geometrically plausible $O/I$ packets of a disabled branch are excluded. The admissible doubled graph contains both signs of every such node and every translation.

The branch label and the old counters in $O_e$, or post counters in $I_e$, determine the source edge; the head anchor determines the position on its corridor. Thus distinct edges cannot share an intermediate ID. Home merging is injective because the actual branch images are disjoint. This is a partial-injective micrograph. Its doubled edge matching exchanges $c^+$ with $c'^-$ for each unsigned edge $c\to c'$, and the phase matching exchanges the two signs at each node.

\section{Literal templates and the new two blocks}\label{fpa:pi:sec:templates}
We now give the full finite family, so the construction does not depend on an unspecified particle mechanism. Every displayed pair $P_g,Q_g$ is a pair of occupied supports, understood as binary words on the type-specific interval $W_g=I_{B_g}(0)$. Pair types have $B_g=B_2$; triple types have $B_g=b$. The invariant template anchor is zero in the display. It need not be the head's leftmost site or an occupied site in both endpoints.

\subsection{Edge templates}
For each moving mode $u$, let $w$ be its forward direction ($v$ for $O_e$, $-v$ for $I_e$). There is one free pair type:
\begin{equation}\label{fpa:pi:eq:free}
 \{0,d(u^+)\}\longleftrightarrow\{w,w+d(u^-)\}.
\end{equation}
Its invariant key is the \emph{predecessor} head anchor. Near a marker, there are triple types
\begin{align}
 \{0,wt,wt+d(u^+)\}&\longleftrightarrow
 \{0,w(t+1),w(t+1)+d(u^-)\},&&S\le t\le L,\label{fpa:pi:eq:behind}\\
 \{0,-wt,-wt+d(u^+)\}&\longleftrightarrow
 \{0,-w(t-1),-w(t-1)+d(u^-)\},&&S+1\le t\le L.\label{fpa:pi:eq:ahead}
\end{align}
For each nonzero branch there are dispatch, endpoint, and commit triples:
\begin{align}
 \{0,S,S+d(H_q^+)\}&\longleftrightarrow
 \{0,vS,vS+d(O_e^-)\},\label{fpa:pi:eq:dispatch}\\
 \{0,-vS,-vS+d(O_e^+)\}&\longleftrightarrow
 \{v\Delta,v\Delta-vS,v\Delta-vS+d(I_e^-)\},\label{fpa:pi:eq:endpoint}\\
 \{0,vS,vS+d(I_e^+)\}&\longleftrightarrow
 \{0,S,S+d(H_{q'}^-)\}.\label{fpa:pi:eq:commit}
\end{align}
Dispatch uses $G_e$, endpoint has no contextual guard, and commit uses the exact $I_e$. The endpoint key remains the \emph{old} marker coordinate, even after the marker moves. Each zero-update branch contributes
\begin{equation}\label{fpa:pi:eq:direct}
 \{0,S,S+d(H_q^+)\}\longleftrightarrow
 \{0,S,S+d(H_{q'}^-)\},
\end{equation}
guarded by $G_e=I_e$.

\subsection{Phase templates}
For each moving mode, use the pair
\[
 \{0,d(u^+)\}\longleftrightarrow\{0,d(u^-)\},
\]
and, for $s\in\{-1,+1\}$ and $S\le t\le L$, the triples
\[
 \{0,st,st+d(u^+)\}\longleftrightarrow
 \{0,st,st+d(u^-)\}.
\]
For every control use the home triple
\[
 \{0,S,S+d(H_q^+)\}\longleftrightarrow
 \{0,S,S+d(H_q^-)\}.
\]
All phase types are unguarded.

\subsection{Raw predicates and guard reads}
For a type with write radius $B_g$, put $\ell_g=3B_g+1$. Its new raw predicate at $u$ requires that all particles in $I_{\ell_g}(u)$ be exactly $u+P_g$ or $u+Q_g$, and that its old contextual guard, if any, hold. We omit the old \emph{same-type raw-key exclusion}. The new all-type isolation and prospective test replace that safety mechanism; the template list is no longer an execution order.

At home interactions the counter classes are read at
\[
 u-(Z+k),\quad u+(Z+k),\qquad 0\le k\le J.
\]
One occupied position selects a finite class, all-zero reads mean $>J$, and multiple occupied positions make the guard false. These reads lie outside the type's write support. The endpoint swap leaves them untouched, and it preserves exactness in $I_{\ell_g}(u)$, so every raw predicate is endpoint-symmetric.

For the edge family take $r_E=Z+J$; this dominates every exactness and guard read. For the phase family take $r_P=3b+1=K$. Lemma~\ref{fpa:pi:lem:parallel} gives the two full-shift involutions $\parE,\parP$, each using the common write bound $b$. Empty edge families give the identity block. Sources with no nonzero branch are covered without adding fictitious moving modes.

\section{Preservation on every admissible state}\label{fpa:pi:sec:preservation}
\subsection{Unique recognition}
Every admissible state has one unique pair of particles at distance at most $D$: its head. Markers have mutual separation at least $Z$; head-to-marker distances are at least $S-D=D+2>D$. Every endpoint template has exactly one close pair and no other distance at most $D$. Therefore a raw match must use the actual head pair. Its globally unique gap fixes its signed mode, and a triple match fixes the relevant marker. No background marker pair or head-marker pair can replace the head.

The diameter of a triple endpoint is at most $2b<Z$, so two different markers cannot both serve as its nearby singleton. For each individual old type there is at most one old raw key on an admissible state. Thus its old same-type exclusion was vacuous there: removing that test introduces no hidden matches on valid states.

The other markers lie outside the intended triple's exactness radius $K$. Ordinary triple keys are markers, at distance at least $Z>K$ from other markers. At a reverse endpoint interaction the key is the old marker location, at distance at most one from the new marker; the other markers are still at least $Z-1>K$ away. Hence the intended triple exactness tests hold.

\subsection{The complete corridor boundary partition}
For a moving mode, orient its corridor in direction $w$. Let $A$ be the departure marker, $C$ the destination marker, and let $x$ be the head anchor. Set
\[
 N=w(C-A)\ge Z,\qquad t=w(x-A),\qquad S\le t\le N-S.
\]
For $O_e$ the corridor length is $Z+c$; for $I_e$ it is $Z+c+\Delta$. The third marker is outside every relevant near-contact range, and $N>2(L+1)$.

For a plus state the free key is $u=x$. Pair exactness holds exactly when $L<t<N-L$. The plus cases are the disjoint exhaustive partition
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{ll}\toprule
Candidate & Oriented head position\\\midrule
Behind triple & $S\le t\le L$\\
Free pair & $L+1\le t\le N-L-1$\\
Ahead triple & $N-L\le t\le N-S-1$\\
Forward interaction & $t=N-S$\\\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
For a minus state the free inverse key is $u=x-w$, whose oriented coordinate is $t-1$. Pair exactness holds exactly when $L<t-1<N-L$. The minus partition is
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{ll}\toprule
Candidate & Oriented head position\\\midrule
Reverse interaction & $t=S$\\
Behind inverse triple & $S+1\le t\le L+1$\\
Free inverse pair & $L+2\le t\le N-L$\\
Ahead inverse triple & $N-L+1\le t\le N-S$\\\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Both tables include the equality cases. A behind triple from distance $L$ reaches minus distance $L+1$, but the free predecessor key still sees its marker at distance $L$, so a free inverse cannot compete. An ahead free move from marker distance $L+1$ reaches minus distance $L$, but there is no ahead triple with predecessor distance $L+1$. At forward arrival distance $S$ there is no further travel, and no travel from behind distance $S-1$ enters a reverse interaction. These facts hold for both physical directions; the head anchor convention has not been reflected.

\subsection{Every interaction and every guard}
At home plus, only outgoing dispatch/direct types of that control can match. Their domain predicates are disjoint and test the actual counters, so there is one candidate exactly when the source ID has a successor. At home minus, only incoming commit/direct reverse endpoints can match. Their \emph{image} predicates are disjoint and include natural pre-counter existence, so there is one candidate exactly when that ID has a predecessor. Using a forward predicate at a reverse commit would invalidate this conclusion.

At the outbound forward wall, the endpoint rewrite is anchored at old marker $m$, and produces marker $m'=m+v\Delta$ and inbound-minus head anchor $m'-vS$. The reverse endpoint is necessarily anchored at
\[
 m'-v\Delta=m,
\]
because its singleton offset is $v\Delta$ and its head offset is $v\Delta-vS$. The same type-anchor key survives the swap, even when the actual marker moves.

At the outbound reverse wall, the old counters satisfy $G_e$ because this intermediate belongs to an enabled edge; reverse dispatch returns to its genuine home-plus predecessor. At the inbound forward wall, the post counters satisfy $I_e$ for the same reason; commit reaches its genuine home-minus target. At the inbound reverse wall, the endpoint inverse restores the old marker and outbound-plus head. Branch-specific signed gaps eliminate all other interaction types, and the corridor partitions eliminate travel competition.

It follows that every admissible edge-block input has zero or one raw key globally. A present swap remains admissible and leaves exactly the same raw type-anchor key, with its orientation reversed. A missing incident microedge leaves no raw key. This argument covers arbitrary counters and all enabled branches, not just a sampled forward orbit.

\subsection{The phase block and agreement theorem}
At a home, its unique phase triple flips sign. At a moving node, if every marker is farther than $L$ from the unchanged head anchor, the phase pair is the unique match. Otherwise there is one marker at distance $S,\ldots,L$, and its unique phase triple is the match. The marker and anchor geometry do not change during a phase flip, so exactly the same key remains, with opposite orientation.

A singleton raw-key set automatically passes all-type isolation. Since the corresponding swap preserves that singleton set, it also passes prospectivity. Empty edge sets remain empty. Thus $\parE$ equals the old edge matching and $\parP$ equals the old sign flip on every admissible signed node. Both maps preserve admissibility. Their composition and inverse therefore agree with the old rule and inverse throughout that set, proving the simulation part of Theorem~\ref{fpa:pi:thm:main}. The source-uniform geometric audit is supplied as \file{scientific/audit-preservation.md}.

\section{Clocks observations and time symmetry}\label{fpa:pi:sec:clocks}
\subsection{Time reversal and the exact path}
Since both new blocks are full-shift involutions,
\[
 \Fnew^{-1}=\parE\parP,
 \qquad \parE\Fnew\parE=\Fnew^{-1},
 \qquad \parP\Fnew\parP=\Fnew^{-1}.
\]
Each displayed time reverser is itself binary and number-conserving. This statement is immediate algebraically from the proved factorization; it does not attribute novelty to that algebraic architecture.

On an admissible plus node with successor, $\Fnew$ advances one unsigned microedge and retains plus. On a minus node with predecessor it moves backward one microedge and retains minus. At a missing edge it changes sign in one step. There is no extra external phase schedule: the composition defines one time-independent CA step.

For a nonzero branch at selected old counter $c$, the two travel lengths are $Z+c-2S$ and $Z+c+\Delta-2S$. Adding dispatch, endpoint, and commit gives
\begin{equation}\label{fpa:pi:eq:clock}
 \tau_e(c)=3+2(Z+c)+\Delta-4S.
\end{equation}
A zero-update edge takes one step. Equality of admissible trajectories therefore preserves the exact old clocks, not only reachability or an asymptotic slowdown.

\wtag\ Equation~\eqref{fpa:pi:eq:clock} is Report~15's clock~\eqref{fpa:eq:clock}; it is unchanged because the two rules agree on admissible states.

If the initial ID is predecessor-free and its forward path has $T$ microedges, its reflected orbit has exactly $2T+2$ states. Partial injectivity prevents an earlier repetition from a predecessor-free start. At halt the state reflects; it does not freeze or enter a separate attractor. Nonfinal stuck IDs also reflect. Consequently periodicity is equivalent to finite forward path length, and becomes equivalent to designated halting only with the additional premise that every nonhalting input has an infinite source path.

\subsection{Halt observer and clean exact targets}
The anchored halt word on $[0,S+D]$ has ones exactly at $0,S,S+d(H_h^+)$ and zeros elsewhere. Its length remains
\[
 S+D+1=3D+3.
\]
On a valid orbit its occurrence is equivalent to forward reachability of the halt control. No backward traversal can introduce a new halt home, since that would require an outgoing edge from a halt control that has no exits.

For a clean whole-configuration target, assume the original source has a control-wide no-incoming start $q_0$ and a no-outgoing halt $h$. Use forward controls $F(q)$, backward controls $B(q)$, and a new terminal $H$. Include each original branch forward and its inverse backward with exact image guard, plus the zero-update bridges $F(h)\to B(h)$ and $B(q_0)\to H$. Domains and images exchange on reverse branches; the two empty boundary slots make the bridges compatible with partial injectivity. The wrapped source has
\[
 m'=2m+1,\qquad p'=2p,\qquad a'=2a+2,
\]
and the same cut $J$. All geometry constants must be recomputed for this enlarged source.

On input $(a_0,b_0)$ the target is $\operatorname{Enc}(H,a_0,b_0)^+$. It is computable and input-dependent, not a universal constant configuration. If $\Theta$ is the original forward physical microedge count with the \emph{enlarged} geometry, then
\[
 \tau(c,\Delta)=\tau(c+\Delta,-\Delta)
\]
and the first clean-target time is $2\Theta+2$. The full reflected period is $4\Theta+6$. For the bundled one-increment clean-target example, $D=18$, $S=38$, $Z=782$, $\Theta=1416$, first target time $2834$, and reflected period $5670$. The new radius is $3507$; the five-particle orbit itself is unchanged.

The classical reversible-counter sources and their normalization belong to the inherited compiler argument, not a new source theorem in this report. Its proof and schema are included as references so these dependency boundaries remain auditable.

\section{Radius and finite resources}\label{fpa:pi:sec:resources}
Applying Lemma~\ref{fpa:pi:lem:parallel} independently gives
\begin{align*}
 R_E&=3(b+Z+J)=33b+30+9J,\\
 R_P&=3(b+3b+1)=12b+3.
\end{align*}
A composition has radius bounded by the sum, hence
\begin{equation}\label{fpa:pi:eq:newradius}
 R_{\mathrm{new}}\le45b+33+9J=180D+258+9J.
\end{equation}
These are sufficient radii. The new bound is not pointwise smaller for every tiny source: for a two-control, one zero-update branch example with $D=4$ and $J=0$, the old bound is $540$ and the new bound is $978$. The improvement claimed for the universal ledger is a comparison of sufficient bounds for that source. No proof that either bound is minimal is offered. A sharper special-purpose separation argument could conceivably improve them.

The literal family still has
\[
 8pD+29p+m+a
\]
endpoint types, where $a$ is the number of zero-update branches. In the old construction this counted ordered factors: $4p$ pair gates, $8pD+23p+m$ unguarded triples, and $2p+a$ contextual triples. Here the same data count template alternatives inside \emph{two} parallel involutions. The inherited ledger fields named \file{E}, \file{P}, and \file{factors} retain type counts, not a new ordered runtime sequence.

The old sufficient radius was
\begin{align}\label{fpa:pi:eq:oldradius}
 R_{\mathrm{old}}={}&4p(6D+8)+(8pD+23p+m)(24D+32)\notag\\
 &+(2p+a)(Z+J+12D+16).
\end{align}
The new bound depends on geometric neighborhoods rather than summing a radius once per executed template. This does not remove template dependence from the Boolean computation of candidacy or prospectivity.

\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{lrr}\toprule
Source or substitution & Old bound & New bound\\\midrule
Two-control increment, $D=8$, $J=0$ & $21\,144$ & $1\,698$\\
Clean-target sample, $D=18$, $J=1$ & $164\,314$ & $3\,507$\\
Universal ledger, $D=509\,508$, $J=0$
 & $3\,292\,955\,588\,459\,274\,804$ & $91\,711\,698$\\\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The universal row substitutes the frozen Report 19 ledger
\[
 m=122\,622,\quad p=66\,066,\quad a=75\,495,\quad J=0
\]
into source-uniform formulas. The original ledger and its provenance are supplied as inert evidence. It has $269\,291\,358\,255$ endpoint types, and that array is \emph{not} materialized here. The symbolic full local truth table would have $2^{2R+1}$ entries at a chosen sufficient radius, but no such table is emitted. The reference interpreter constructs small-source arrays for transparency and does not provide a universal-efficient new-rule evaluator.

\section{Malformed inputs and why prospectivity matters}\label{fpa:pi:sec:counterexamples}
For controls $q,h$, a true-guard right increment, and $J=0$, consider
\[
 X=\{-118,-112,0,18,23\}.
\]
Each new block initially has one raw candidate. Its one hypothetical swap would create an additional raw key, so the prospective test rejects it. Both new blocks fix $X$, hence $\Fnew(X)=X$. The frozen old ordered rule instead gives
\[
 \Fold(X)=\{-119,-113,0,19,24\}.
\]
The checker also confirms that the old edge and phase products separately fail to square to the identity on this same $X$. This explicit distinction rules out treating the old products as full-shift matchings and merely assigning them smaller radius bounds.

An independent tiny counterexample shows why isolation alone is insufficient. On the binary ring of period $12$, take one type with supports
\[
 P=\{-1,0\},\qquad Q=\{-1,1\},\qquad b=r=1,\quad H=4.
\]
Require exactness in radius one, but omit prospectivity. The input occupied sites $\{0,1,2\}$ move to $\{0,1,3\}$, which the next isolation-only update leaves fixed. The first update creates a conflicting key; the update is not an involution. The prospective rule rejects the problematic move and remains involutive. Exhaustive periodic enumeration rediscovers this mutation counterexample rather than assuming it as an expected output.

\section{Executable evidence and its limits}\label{fpa:pi:sec:tests}
\subsection{Reference interpreter and checks}
\file{scientific/parallel_particles.py} implements finite-support versions of both new blocks. Its separately written coordinate-local routine queries only the declared neighborhood. Before/after comparisons use complete type-anchor key sets and discard only endpoint orientation.
