Verification raises explicit exceptions for failures of mass conservation, candidate-set invariance, selected-set invariance, selected-orientation reversal, and block involutivity. These checks remain active under Python optimization.

The supported construction entry point accepts the frozen validated JSON source interface. Its compiler and block records are immutable under ordinary Python attribute/container operations; finite supports require exact built-in sets or frozensets of exact integers, and runtime flags require exact Booleans. Custom predicates accepted by the lower-level generic helper must independently satisfy the read-radius, purity, and endpoint-symmetry contract. The source-uniform theorem uses only the frozen compiler's validated guards. Deliberate low-level reflection or monkey-patching is outside the ordinary immutability claim.

\subsection{Finite results}
The primary suite compares against an independently generated micro-path and checks:
\begin{itemize}
\item $8928$ admissible doubled states across $12$ source/input cases, including $8450$ states in complete reflected cycles and the entire $5670$-state clean-target cycle;
\item left/right increments and decrements, zero-update branches, stuck homes, disjoint guarded mergers, exact clocks, and all free/contact equality cases;
\item $89$ direct comparisons with the frozen old eager CA on valid states;
\item all $4096$ subsets of two specified eleven-site malformed universes, block verification, and whole-map inverse checks;
\item $100$ seeded noisy or multihead inputs, $100$ translation checks, and $2074$ comparisons with the declared-radius coordinate-local routine;
\item a configuration with two simultaneously selected edge moves and two simultaneously selected phase moves, plus the malformed cascade above.
\end{itemize}
The independent periodic suite exhausts $24\,576$ binary words across three small families, with the same number of coordinate-local oracle comparisons. It checks candidate and selected-key invariance, mass, and involution. Nontrivial families include $520$ words with multiple simultaneous moves, a contextual guard, and dense malformed backgrounds. The deliberately conflicting two-type family acts as identity, a limitation explicitly disclosed in its receipt. The public-interface suite checks $58$ malformed-input or mutation rejections and ordinary immutability.

All suites are replayed normally and with \file{python -O}. The receipt files record exact case details. These finite tests are corroboration, not exhaustive enumeration of the astronomical source-uniform neighborhood, and neither the proof nor the audits are proof-assistant formalizations.

\subsection{What transfers to arithmetic certificates}
On a computation certified to remain in the admissible encoding, equality of old and new trajectories transports its \emph{semantic statement}: the same encoded source run, observer event, time, or clean target is true of $\Fnew$. This transport must explicitly invoke the admissible-state theorem and the original certificate's hypotheses.

Report 19's lazy evaluator still implements the old ordered rule on arbitrary malformed finite inputs. It cannot automatically be relabeled as an evaluator of this new rule. Likewise Report 20's ordered-factor event-budget certificate and arbitrary-malformed-state transition fibers do not become new-rule certificates by replacing a radius or a title. The malformed counterexample proves that a blanket identification would be false.

A literal new-rule arithmetic verifier must encode its own raw-key isolation, hypothetical-swap key preservation, and simultaneous update, or restrict itself to a proved admissible semantic transport theorem. This report does not provide that compiler, enumerate its fibers, or establish cheap arithmetic gate counts. Fewer composed CA blocks and a smaller sufficient spatial radius alone imply no template-independent running-time or Diophantine-witness-size bound.

\wtag\ Report~19 is Part~\ref{fpa:part:four} and Report~20 is Part~\ref{fpa:part:five}. The two missing pieces this subsection names were supplied the same day by the next two Reports of this Part: Report~27's sparse new-rule evaluator (Theorem~\ref{fpa:sp:thm:main}) and Report~28's new-rule arithmetic compiler, which encodes raw-key isolation, hypothetical-swap rediscovery and the simultaneous update at each fixed horizon (Theorem~\ref{fpa:pq:thm:main}).

\section{Relationship to prior work}\label{fpa:pi:sec:prior}
The architecture of parallel reversible local rewrites and products of involutions is established prior art. Four primary sources are especially close.

Boyle, Lind, and Rudolph describe marker automorphisms of shifts of finite type using nonoverlapping $MDM\mapsto M\pi(D)M$ replacements in Section 2, page 74~\cite{fpa:blr}. Their Theorem 2.6, pages 76--77, uses a product of two marker involutions. This precedes both marker-based parallel permutations and a two-involution composition as general ideas. Our candidate family has no separately installed marker word; its raw-key prospectivity is the particular local safety criterion proved here.

Toffoli and Margolus describe conserved-landscape permutations in Section 5.3 and local conservative block permutations in Section 5.5 of their review~\cite{fpa:tm}. Their recognition-preservation principle is a direct conceptual predecessor: the inverse must recognize the same local operation. The binary flip example itself is not number-conserving. We cite section numbers because the inspected author-uploaded reprint includes later corrections and annotations.

Gajardo, Kari, and Moreira characterize time-symmetric cellular automata as exactly products of two CA involutions, Proposition 1~\cite{fpa:gkm}. The algebraic time-reversal identities in Section~\ref{fpa:pi:sec:clocks} are an instance of that characterization, not a new architecture. Full reversibility alone does not justify an arbitrary two-block factorization; our two individual involution proofs are the substantive extra hypotheses.

Maldonado, Moreira, and Gajardo give a universal time-symmetric number-conserving CA~\cite{fpa:mmg}. Their Proposition 2 constructs a globally reversible conservative extension of a reversible partitioned CA by invariant recognized left, right, and malformed classes. The radius-one pair update $\overline A_f$ fixes its malformed class $W$. This statement concerns that pair update, not the complete composition $\overline A_f\circ\overline I$. Their Theorem 1 and corollaries yield the stated time-symmetric conservative universality, with an expanded alphabet; the associated time-reversing involution need not itself conserve numeric mass.

The specific result of this report is the complete-key prospective-isolation lemma with radius $3(b+r)$ and its exact application to the pinned binary five-particle compiler, including the admissible boundary and guard proof and explicit new radius. Both factors here individually conserve the binary particle number. These distinctions describe what is proved; they do not establish global priority. No novelty or optimality conclusion is drawn from this focused literature check. The primary-source audit and links are frozen with the packet.

\wtag\ \textbf{Further lineage (added in the write; not checked at page level).} Two further traditions bear on this section. Report~26's primary-source audit checked Kari's two block-permutation papers at abstract level only and did not consult the other works named here. Reversible partitioned cellular automata and block representations: Morita and Harao~\cite{fpa:moritaharao}; Kari's representation of reversible cellular automata by block permutations~\cite{fpa:kari96,fpa:kari99}; Durand-Lose's representation by reversible block cellular automata~\cite{fpa:durandlose}. Number-conserving cellular automata: Boccara and Fuk\'s~\cite{fpa:boccarafuks}; Durand, Formenti and R\'oka~\cite{fpa:dfr}; Moreira~\cite{fpa:moreira03}; and Morita and Imai~\cite{fpa:moritaimai2001}, which Part~\ref{fpa:part:one} already cites. They are named to place the construction, not as sources of the prospective test. The M2 placement's reading is that Lemma~\ref{fpa:pi:lem:parallel} is an elementary specialization of the conserved-landscape principle (a simultaneous update by nonoverlapping local involutions is an involution when each preserves its own triggering landscape); its added content is the per-candidate hypothetical-swap test, the invariance of rejected keys' eligibility, and the explicit constants $H=2(b+r)$ and $3(b+r)$. Report~26's audit is shipped as \path{26-parallel-involutions-references-prior-art-audit.md}.

\section{Portable package and verification}\label{fpa:pi:sec:package}
The companion ZIP contains this editable LaTeX source and rendered PDF; the sealed scientific packet, including independent audits, normal and optimized producer receipts, and the old compiler; inherited compiler/schema and universal-ledger references; the primary-source attribution audit; and standard-library-only release verification and replay scripts.

From an extracted release directory, run
\begin{quote}\small\ttfamily
python3 -B verify\_release.py\\
python3 -B replay.py --output /an/existing/parent/new-output-directory
\end{quote}
The output directory must be new and outside the release. Replay verifies the mandatory seal before running code, makes independent fresh normal and optimized copies, places logs and receipts outside those copies, and checks exact inventory, bytes, modes, and modification times after every executed script. Python bytecode writing is disabled. Scientific suites execute with the interpreter's isolated mode and site packages disabled. No upstream repository checkout, network request, software installation, or universal source execution is needed.

The exact-inventory manifest and mandatory source pins are complementary. The inventory rejects added or missing files and directories, symlinks, unsafe paths, duplicate JSON keys, and changed bytes. The hardcoded scientific pins prevent a missing scientific source from being silently accepted after merely resealing an edited inventory. Negative tamper tests exercise missing and changed dependencies, altered pins, malformed seals, extra files/directories, symlinks, unsafe output locations, and source-mutation detection. The PDF build command writes into a separate build directory; TeX and Poppler are optional only for rebuilding and visual checking the already supplied PDF.

The seal is an internal consistency check, not an external digital signature. An attacker able to replace both the verifier and the seal can forge a self-consistent package. The final archive digest must therefore be compared through a trusted delivery channel. Replay also does not turn bounded tests into a proof of the universal statement; that role belongs to the arguments in this report and the frozen audits.

\section{Further questions}\label{fpa:rep:26:last}\label{fpa:pi:sec:questions}
Several concrete next steps follow from the present result.
\begin{enumerate}
\item Can type-specific support geometry reduce $H=2(b+r)$ or avoid paying the entire contextual guard radius for candidates that cannot compete? Any reduction must still control cooperative raw-key births and changes in initially rejected eligibility tests.
\item Can a lazy evaluator enumerate all potentially competing type-anchor keys and all prospective changes without materializing the template array? Completeness of the candidate search, especially on malformed multihead inputs, is the central obligation.
\item Can one compile the new local selection mechanism into an explicit arithmetic certificate with source-uniform size bounds? The certificate must specify whether it covers arbitrary full-shift neighborhoods or only admissible encoded computations.
\item Can the general lemma and the signed corridor partition be formalized in a proof assistant? The important distinctions are type identity versus orientation, predecessor anchor versus current head anchor, and read windows versus actual write supports.
\item Which other conservative finite-particle simulations admit the same singleton-key property on their intended encodings? The general lemma alone does not guarantee that intended dynamics survive the stronger filter.
\end{enumerate}
\wtag\ (3~October 2026.) Question~2 is answered by Report~27 (Theorem~\ref{fpa:sp:thm:main}): complete sparse discovery of every competing type-anchor key, on malformed and multihead supports too, with at most $n+2$ raw-discovery calls per step and no template array. Question~3 is answered at each fixed horizon by Report~28 (Theorem~\ref{fpa:pq:thm:main}): a degree-four certificate on arbitrary finite supports of a fixed mass, malformed ones included, with source-uniform resource ledgers; its witness arity grows affinely with the horizon, so no unknown-horizon certificate is supplied. Questions~1, 4 and~5 remain open. For Question~1, a randomized probe run at placement on small synthetic families found no counterexample with $H$ reduced to $2(b+r)-1$ or to $b+r$; that is weak evidence, not a proof.

These are open development directions here, not completed improvements. The completed conclusion is a rigorously defined binary full-shift replacement whose two individually conservative involutions preserve the entire intended five-particle dynamics with the explicit sufficient radius~\eqref{fpa:pi:eq:newradius}.

\reportopening{27}{Sparse evaluation of a two involution particle cellular automaton}{}{Research Report 27 \quad Exact finite support evaluator and reproducibility packet}

\begin{reportabstract}{27}
We give an exact finite-support evaluator for the new binary, number-conserving cellular automaton of Report 26. Its two whole-family involutions are evaluated by complete sparse discovery, all-type isolation, prospective preservation of raw keys, and simultaneous rewriting. The evaluator agrees with the eager new-rule interpreter on every finite support, including malformed configurations. Compilation stores source and explicit class metadata, without allocating the potentially enormous endpoint family. An $n$-particle forward or inverse step uses at most $n+2$ raw-discovery calls, or $2n+4$ with optional verification; there is no ordered cascade. We prove discovery completeness and give operation, bit, and space bounds, exposing the quadratic-in-cutoff class expansion. On the frozen 122,622-control source, the evaluator executes 128 literal startup steps and 128 malformed cases although the symbolic family has 269,291,358,255 types. These are bounded checks, not a completed universal startup, a Turing-machine step, or a halting result. No new Diophantine compiler or arithmetic gate count is supplied.
\end{reportabstract}

\wtag\ Delivered as \path{Sparse_Parallel_Particle_Evaluation_Package.zip} (batch~82, archive~18; arrival \texttt{db37d18c8}), dated 3~October 2026; files placed by \texttt{7d2b1b245} with the prefix \path{27-sparse-parallel-}. Report~27 evaluates the rule of Report~26 (Section~\ref{fpa:rep:26:first}ff.) and answers its second question. Its Lemma~\ref{fpa:sp:lem:parallel} restates Report~26's Lemma~\ref{fpa:pi:lem:parallel} with a condensed proof, and its malformed witness is Report~26's (notes at both places). The 32,034,272-byte universal source and the two universal traces it ships are not shipped here; the README rebuilds them byte for byte. Report~27's macros \verb|\code| and \verb|\C| are \verb|\file| and \verb|\calC| here; nothing printed changed. Its statements were numbered $1,2,\dots$ through the Report; here they are numbered by section, and its appendix is an ordinary section.

\section{Result and semantic boundary}\label{fpa:rep:27:first}
Report 26 replaces the old ordered local-factor dynamics by a \emph{new} rule $\Phi=P_{\parallel}\circ E_{\parallel}$. Each displayed block is an involution on the full binary shift $\{0,1\}^{\Z}$, preserves finite particle number, and commutes with the unit shift. The new and old rules agree on the entire admissible doubled source micrograph, but differ on malformed inputs. This report supplies an implementation of the new rule for arbitrary finite supports $X\subset\Z$, not just promised valid encodings.

The implementation is \file{scientific/sparse_parallel.py}. The inherited immutable source metadata, arithmetic endpoint lookup, incidence lists, and guard tables are reused; legacy ordered execution is not used. In particular, \file{compiler.step} evaluates the new rule, whereas \file{compiler.metadata.step} is an inherited \emph{old-rule} method and is not the supported execution entry point here. The ledger field \file{factors} counts endpoint types, not sequential operations of the new rule.

\begin{theorem}[Exact sparse finite-support evaluation]\label{fpa:sp:thm:main}
For every source accepted by the frozen source schema and every finite set of integer positions $X$, the supported sparse step equals the eager new-rule map $\Phi(X)$. The inverse option equals $\Phi^{-1}(X)$. No endpoint array or ordered type cascade is required. If $n=|X|$, each step makes at most $2+2\lfloor n/2\rfloor\le n+2$ raw-discovery calls; optional verification increases this to at most $4+4\lfloor n/2\rfloor\le2n+4$.
\end{theorem}

The full-shift CA theorem is inherited from Report 26 and restated below. The implementation theorem concerns finite-support execution. Neither finite testing nor a roundtrip check alone proves equality of different evaluators. The proof in Sections~\ref{fpa:sp:sec:discovery}--\ref{fpa:sp:sec:cost} establishes that equality; the tests are corroboration. All earlier delivered reports remain unchanged.

\section{Source data and endpoint geometry}
\subsection{Accepted sources and expanded metadata}
The schema \file{reversible-two-counter-v1} represents a finite reversible two-counter partial transition system. It contains an ordered list of $m$ distinct controls, designated start and halt controls, cutoff $J\ge0$, and an ordered list of branches. A branch has a unique name, source and target control, side $v\in\{-1,1\}$ identifying counter 0 or 1, update $\delta\in\{-1,0,1\}$, and a Boolean guard built from true, equality, greater-than, conjunction, disjunction, and negation. Every atom has a threshold $k\ge0$ with $k\le J$ and, on the updated counter, $k+\delta\le J$. Enabled updates cannot make a counter negative; domains at each source and exact images at each target are pairwise disjoint. The halt has no exits.

Let $p$ count nonzero-update branches and $a$ count zero-update branches, in their order-preserving partitions; put $t=p+a$. The implementation validates guards on the classes $0,\ldots,J,>J$, materializing domain and image tables with $(J+2)^2$ entries per branch. Thus ``source-sized'' includes \emph{expanded class descriptors}. It does not mean polynomial time or space in the binary input length of $J$.

The construction constants and type counts are
\begin{align*}
D&=2m+4p,& S&=2D+2,& B_2&=D+1,\\
L&=3D+4,& b=B_3&=4D+5,& Z&=10b+10+2J,\\
N_E&=p(4D+15)+a,& N_P&=p(4D+14)+m,\\
F&=N_E+N_P=8pD+29p+m+a.&&&
\end{align*}
Here $L$ is the pair travel/exactness constant, not a common exactness radius for all templates. A template with support parameter $B_g$ has its own exactness radius $\ell_g=3B_g+1$: $\ell_g=L$ for pairs and $\ell_g=12D+16$ for triples.

\subsection{Literal endpoint families}
Controls have indices $q=0,\ldots,m-1$ and moving branches $e=0,\ldots,p-1$. The signed mode gaps are
\begin{align*}
d(H_q,+)&=2q+1,& d(H_q,-)&=2q+2,\\
d(O_e,+)&=2m+4e+1,&d(O_e,-)&=2m+4e+2,\\
d(I_e,+)&=2m+4e+3,&d(I_e,-)&=2m+4e+4.
\end{align*}
All gaps lie in $[1,D]$, are distinct, and odd means plus. Write
\[
A(M,\sigma,h)=\{h,h+d(M,\sigma)\},\qquad
T(M,\sigma,h)=\{0\}\cup A(M,\sigma,h).
\]
Each row below gives a pair of endpoint sets at the invariant gate anchor zero. Translating both by $u$ gives anchor $u$. In moving modes $M=O_e,I_e$, set $w=v$ for outbound and $w=-v$ for inbound.

\begin{longtable}{@{}p{0.15\textwidth}p{0.33\textwidth}p{0.41\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
Family & First endpoint & Second endpoint \\ \midrule
\endhead
Free & $A(M,+,0)$ & $A(M,-,w)$ \\
Behind & $T(M,+,wk)$ & $T(M,-,w(k+1))$, $S\le k\le L$ \\
Ahead & $T(M,+,-wk)$ & $T(M,-,-w(k-1))$, $S+1\le k\le L$ \\
Dispatch & $T(H_{q_s},+,S)$ & $T(O_e,-,vS)$ \\
Endpoint & $T(O_e,+,-vS)$ & $\{v\delta\}\cup A(I_e,-,v\delta-vS)$ \\
Commit & $T(I_e,+,vS)$ & $T(H_{q_t},-,S)$ \\
Direct & $T(H_{q_s},+,S)$ & $T(H_{q_t},-,S)$ \\
Phase free & $A(M,+,0)$ & $A(M,-,0)$ \\
Phase near & $T(M,+,\eta k)$ & $T(M,-,\eta k)$, $\eta=\pm1$, $S\le k\le L$ \\
Phase home & $T(H_q,+,S)$ & $T(H_q,-,S)$ \\ \bottomrule
\end{longtable}
Free, behind, ahead, dispatch, endpoint, commit, and direct are $E$ types. The three phase families are $P$ types. Dispatch and direct carry the branch domain guard; commit carries its exact image guard; the other types have no class guard. Pair templates have $B_g=B_2$, all triples have $B_g=b$. The two endpoints of a type are distinct and are not translates of one another. All endpoint particles lie in $[-b,b]$.

Arithmetic lookup in \file{gate_at(i)} decodes the existing ordered partition into these families without scanning preceding types or a travel interval. Moving $E$ types come in blocks of $4D+15$: two travel modes of $2D+6$ types each, then dispatch, endpoint, commit. Direct types follow. Moving $P$ modes each have $2D+7$ types: phase free and $2(D+3)$ near phases; home phases follow. E identities are $0,\ldots,N_E-1$; P identities are offset by $N_E$. These integers are stable \emph{names}, not an execution schedule.

For reference, a valid home encoding is
\[
\{-Z-c_0,\ 0,\ Z+c_1,\ S,\ S+d(H_q,\sigma)\},\qquad c_0,c_1\in\mathbb N.
\]
The admissible set also includes enabled-edge intermediate encodings and all translations. The sparse algorithm never assumes its input lies in this set.

\section{The new parallel rule}
Put $I_s(u)=[u-s,u+s]\cap\Z$ for $s\ge0$, and let the block read bound satisfy $r\ge b$. The type-specific write interval is $W_g=I_{B_g}(0)$, translated to $u+W_g$. A type-anchor key $(g,u)$ is raw when the particles in $I_{\ell_g}(u)$ are exactly one translated endpoint of type $g$, and its contextual guard holds. The orientation $\lambda\in\{0,1\}$ is the endpoint label, stored separately. Distinct types at one anchor are distinct keys.

A guard reads the two intervals
\[
[u-Z-J,u-Z],\qquad[u+Z,u+Z+J].
\]
An empty interval represents class $J+1$, a singleton represents its exact signed offset from distance $Z$, and two or more particles reject the guard. Its table is the branch domain table or, for commit, the exact image table. These sites lie outside the gate write interval and are unchanged by an endpoint swap.

For a block, write $\calC(X)$ for its complete raw-key set. Let $\tau_k$ be the local involution exchanging the two equal-weight endpoint words on the type's write interval and fixing other words. For a raw key $k=(g,u)$, its hypothetical support $Y_k=\tau_kX$ is obtained by deleting the current endpoint particles and adding the other endpoint particles. Set $H=2(b+r)$. Select $k$ precisely when
\begin{enumerate}
\item $k\in\calC(X)$;
\item no other key of $\calC(X)$ has anchor within distance $H$ of $u$;
\item $\calC(Y_k)$ and $\calC(X)$ have identical keys anchored within distance $b+r$ of $u$.
\end{enumerate}
Condition 3 ignores orientation but retains \emph{every} raw type, including keys failing isolation or prospectivity. All decisions use the same $X$; only then are all selected rewrites performed simultaneously. E and P use separate within-family tests, with
\[
r_E=Z+J,\qquad r_P=3b+1,\qquad H_E=2(b+r_E),\quad H_P=2(b+r_P).
\]
The old raw radius $B_g$, old same-type exclusion, and ordered refresh-and-continue evaluator are not used.

\begin{lemma}[Prospective isolation, Report 26]\label{fpa:sp:lem:parallel}
For finitely many equal-weight local endpoint swaps of write radius at most $b$ with translation-covariant, endpoint-symmetric candidate predicates of read radius at most $r$, the above selected simultaneous map is a full-shift, translation-equivariant, particle-conserving involution of radius at most $3(b+r)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Selected anchors are more than $H$ apart, so their writes are disjoint. A swap at $u$ can affect a raw predicate at $v$ only for $|v-u|\le b+r$. Thus prospectivity is equality of the entire raw-key set after that one swap. A predicate read window cannot meet two selected write windows, since that would put their anchors at distance at most $2(b+r)=H$. Every predicate therefore has its original truth value after simultaneous writing, ruling out cooperative candidate births.

The prospective condition of a key reads within radius $b+2r$ of its anchor. Other selected writes are outside this window by separation. Its own swap interchanges the two sets being compared and is involutive; consequently every isolated key, including one rejected by prospectivity, retains its prospective status. The preserved raw-key set preserves isolation, hence the selected-key set is preserved. Reapplying the same disjoint swaps restores the input. Isolation reads radius $H+r$ around a key, while writing a fixed coordinate requires an anchor within $b$. The output radius is $b+H+r=3(b+r)$. Shift covariance and equal-weight disjoint swaps give the remaining conclusions.
\end{proof}

\wtag\ \textbf{A restatement.} Lemma~\ref{fpa:sp:lem:parallel} is Report~26's Lemma~\ref{fpa:pi:lem:parallel}, and its proof is Report~26's proof in condensed form; it is kept as Report~27's own presentation, not as a second route, and no novelty is claimed for it. The radii displayed next are Report~26's (Section~\ref{fpa:pi:sec:resources}, equation~\eqref{fpa:pi:eq:newradius}).

The type-specific write interval is important: unrelated particles outside a pair's own write interval must not be erased merely because they lie inside the common bound $b$. Applying the lemma to E and P gives
\begin{align*}
R_E&=3(b+Z+J)=33b+30+9J,\\
R_P&=3(b+3b+1)=12b+3,\\
R&\le R_E+R_P=180D+258+9J.
\end{align*}
Report 26 also proves agreement with the old dynamics on every admissible doubled micrograph state, including travel-boundary equalities, branch image guards, and reflections. That preservation theorem is bundled unchanged with its two audits. It is not a claim of full-shift equality to the old rule.

\section{Complete sparse candidate discovery}\label{fpa:sp:sec:discovery}
\subsection{Recovering every possible type}
Sort the finite support and enumerate every occupied close pair $(h,h+d)$ with $1\le d\le D$. The gap uniquely identifies mode and sign. Every endpoint contains such a pair; a triple contains exactly one. Indeed its marker is separated from the head by at least $S$, and from the second pair particle by at least $S-D=D+2>D$. This remains true at the reverse endpoint interaction, where marker and pair have both shifted. The whole input may have many heads or close pairs: none are discarded.

For every moving close pair, always emit the free and phase-free types. For every distinct third particle $z$, write $\rho=h-z$ and $\epsilon=0$ for plus, $1$ for minus. Emit the types specified by the following exhaustive tests:
\begin{align*}
\text{behind:}\quad &k=w\rho-\epsilon, & S&\le k\le L,\\
\text{ahead:}\quad &k=-w\rho+\epsilon, & S+1&\le k\le L,\\
\text{phase near:}\quad &k=|\rho|,\quad\eta=\operatorname{sign}(\rho),& S&\le k\le L.
\end{align*}
The four moving interaction cases are
\[
\begin{array}{ccl}
O_e,+&\rho=-vS&\text{endpoint},\\
O_e,-&\rho= vS&\text{dispatch},\\
I_e,+&\rho= vS&\text{commit},\\
I_e,-&\rho=-vS&\text{endpoint}.
\end{array}
\]
For a home close pair $H_q$, if $h-S\in X$, emit its home phase and every outgoing dispatch/direct incidence when plus, or every incoming commit/direct incidence when minus. Source incidence lists are stored once, with $O(t)$ total entries.

\begin{lemma}[Containment completeness]\label{fpa:sp:lem:discovery}
If either translated endpoint of type $g$ is contained in $X$, the sparse type-discovery procedure emits $g$, without any admissibility, exactness, guard, or isolation assumption.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The endpoint's close pair is enumerated and determines its signed mode. A pair endpoint is covered by unconditional free/phase-free emission. For a triple, its distinct marker is enumerated. Substitution into the endpoint table yields exactly the displayed behind, ahead, phase, interaction, or home test, including all equality boundaries. A behind minus endpoint has $\rho=w(k+1)$, and an ahead minus endpoint has $\rho=-w(k-1)$, explaining the sign corrections. A reverse endpoint interaction retains $\rho=-vS$ even though its marker has moved. Home incidences include every matching branch at that control. These cases exhaust both orientations of every family.
\end{proof}

The procedure only proposes identities. It does not assume that the current left pair coordinate is the invariant anchor. This distinction is essential both for a free reverse endpoint and for the moved marker of a reverse interaction.

\subsection{Recovering every raw key and orientation}
Filter proposed identities to the requested family. For each type, materialize its two sorted shapes $Q_0,Q_1$. For each orientation and every occupied site $z$, try
\[
u=z-\min Q_\lambda.
\]
Retain $(g,u)\mapsto\lambda$ exactly when all $u+Q_\lambda$ are occupied, the closed interval $I_{\ell_g}(u)$ contains exactly $|Q_\lambda|$ particles, and the guard holds. Two binary searches count an interval in sorted $X$. The same interval operation implements guard classes without scanning the $J+1$ detector sites.

\begin{proposition}[Raw-map equality]\label{fpa:sp:prop:raw}
The sparse map contains exactly the eager new-rule keys with the same orientations on every finite $X$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
A genuine raw occurrence has a contained endpoint, so Lemma~\ref{fpa:sp:lem:discovery} supplies its type. Its least occupied endpoint site is tried, giving exactly its invariant anchor. The remaining literal endpoint, inclusive exactness, and guard tests are the definition of raw occurrence. Conversely every returned key passes those same tests. Distinct endpoint sets cannot both equal the exact occupied interval at one key, so orientation is unambiguous.
\end{proof}
Optional center/radius arguments filter \emph{returned anchors} inclusively. They do not crop the support used for discovery or guards. This prevents losing candidates whose defining particles lie outside the requested anchor interval. Prospective rediscovery is performed from scratch on the hypothetical support, rather than reusing the original proposed identities.

\section{Selection equality and local execution}
Sort all raw keys by $(u,g)$. A key has another key within distance $H$ if and only if its predecessor or successor in this order is within $H$. Thus the nearest-neighbor test is exactly the all-type isolation test, even when different types share the same anchor.

For each isolated key, swap its endpoints on the unchanged original $X$, discover the complete new raw map, and compare keys anchored within $b+r$ before and after. Keys are compared without orientation, and the implementation additionally checks that the retained key's orientation reversed. Rejected candidates remain in the original raw map as competitors. Once all decisions are fixed, delete/add the selected endpoints simultaneously.

\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{fpa:sp:thm:main}, equality part]
Proposition~\ref{fpa:sp:prop:raw} gives identical original and hypothetical raw maps. Hence nearest-neighbor isolation, prospective key equality, and selected key/orientation maps agree with the eager new-rule interpreter. Lemma~\ref{fpa:sp:lem:parallel} makes selected writes disjoint, so the final support agrees exactly. Applying block equality first to E then to P proves forward equality. Applying it to P then E proves inverse equality because both blocks are involutions. No argument assumes a valid encoding or five particles.
\end{proof}

Each block also exposes a coordinate-local output oracle. To determine output at $i$, it uses only $X\cap I_{3(b+r)}(i)$. Writing anchors lie within $b$ of $i$; competitors lie within $H$ of those anchors, and raw predicates read a further $r$. Thus every required coordinate lies within $b+H+r=3(b+r)$. Prospective reads also fit within this crop. Its candidate queries remain complete for the relevant anchors. This API is a literal local rule oracle; its repeated raw calls are not covered by the whole-step call count below.

A useful malformed witness is $X=\{-118,-112,0,18,23\}$ for a two-control right increment source with $J=0$. Each block initially has one raw key, but its hypothetical swap creates another raw key. Prospectivity rejects both moves, so the new rule fixes $X$. The inherited old rule maps it to $\{-119,-113,0,19,24\}$. This distinguishes the implemented maps rather than merely showing reversible behavior.

\wtag\ This witness is Report~26's (Section~\ref{fpa:pi:sec:counterexamples}); Report~28's certificate accepts $(X,X)$ and rejects the old rule's image on it (Section~\ref{fpa:pq:sec:cascade}).

\section{Work space and bit bounds}\label{fpa:sp:sec:cost}
\subsection{A bound before prospective rejection}
Let $n=|X|$. All keys admitted to the hypothetical-swap stage are mutually more than $H\ge2b$ apart. Their original endpoints occupy disjoint neighborhoods and each uses at least two particles of the same $X$. Their number is therefore at most $\lfloor n/2\rfloor$. This counts \emph{all isolated keys}, not just the ultimately selected ones, so rejected probes are included.

Exactness ensures each hypothetical swap replaces an equal-sized endpoint without colliding with outside particles; it preserves $n$. The final simultaneous block also preserves $n$, so both block inputs and all discovery snapshots in one step have mass $n$. Each unverified block performs one initial raw discovery plus one for every isolated key. This proves the $2+2\lfloor n/2\rfloor$ bound. Tracing does not repeat selection. With verification, each block additionally discovers the final support and selects once more, then checks the reverse write using that computed selected map. This doubles the call bound to $4+4\lfloor n/2\rfloor$. Empty and singleton supports are included.

\subsection{Expanded source and runtime costs}
Let $\Delta$ bound the longest home-in or home-out list; $\Delta\le t$. Let $G$ be the total guard-program/snapshot size, $c=(J+2)^2$, and $M$ denote immutable metadata size including payloads. Compilation stores
\[
O(m+t+G+tc)\ \text{words, plus actual string and integer payloads}.
\]
Class validation takes expected $O(cG+tc+m+t)$ unit operations, with parsing/name costs charged as well. Dense growing conflict masks have a deliberately loose additional $O(tc^2)$ bit-work bound. Large binary-encoded $J$ can therefore be expensive, even though subsequent sparse guards do not scan detector intervals site by site.

For one step let $C$ bound the number of discovered identities over all original, hypothetical, and verification supports, including both families before filtering. Close pairs, third particles, and incidence emissions give
\[
C=O(n^3+n^2\Delta),\qquad Q=1+n^3+n^2\Delta.
\]
The sharper cap $C\le F$ is also valid. If $K$ bounds raw keys in one family, trying at most $2n$ anchors per type gives $K\le2nC$. Under expected constant-time hash access and unit-cost exact integer arithmetic, one raw call takes
\[
T_{\rm raw}=O\!\left(n\log(n+1)+Q+C\log(C+1)+Cn\log(n+1)\right).
\]
Actual name-string copying is charged separately: each materialized template copies only its source names and the needed index digits. The implementation sorts original raw keys, scans at most $K$ keys per isolated probe for its before-set, and constructs one hypothetical support at a time. A safe expected step bound is
\[
O\!\left((n+2)T_{\rm raw}+K\log(K+1)+nK+n^2\right),
\]
with a constant-factor increase for verification. Substituting $C=O(Q)$ and $K=O(nQ)$ gives a polynomial in particle count and explicit source/class parameters without an unconditional $F$ loop or coordinate-span scan. Dense inputs can discover all $F$ types; no worst-case sublinear-in-$F$ claim is made.

Work storage beyond $M$ is safely bounded by $O(n+Q+nC)$ words. The original raw map, one hypothetical map and restricted key sets, and the output support coexist; previous hypothetical maps are discarded. A step trace contains at most $n$ selected triples. There is no persistent materialized-template cache, which could otherwise grow to the entire type family over many steps.

\subsection{Integer lengths and deterministic bounds}
Let $A=\max_{x\in X}|x|$, or zero for empty $X$. Since equal-sized endpoints lie in $[-b,b]$, an arbitrary particle matching moves at most $2b$ per block, hence at most $4b$ over two actual blocks. Hypothetical and verification snapshots obey the same order of magnitude. Anchors and detector reads are bounded by $A+O(b+Z+J)$, with no ordered-$F$ displacement term.

Let $W$ include the bit lengths of that coordinate bound, $F$, $J$, source numeric values, and container indices; let $L_{\rm name}$ bound source-name lengths. Addition, comparison, and integer hashing cost $O(W)$ in a simple bit model; the bounded integer multiplication/division in decoding can be charged $O(W^2)$. The expected-hash claim is not a worst-case constant-time claim about Python dictionaries.

For an intentionally loose deterministic bound, put $U=1+M+n+Q+nC$, dominate a container operation by at most $U$ key comparisons, and charge $O((W+L_{\rm name}+1)^2)$ per scalar operation. Multiplying the displayed runtime operation bound by $U(W+L_{\rm name}+1)^2$ supplies a conservative polynomial bit bound in the expanded parameters. It is not tight or a practical performance estimate, and it does not remove the non-polynomial-in-$\log J$ class expansion. Compilation's separate mask and parsing costs still apply.

\section{Supported API and reproducibility contract}
Create \file{a = SparseParallelCompiler(source)} from a validated decoded JSON object. Use \file{a.step(X)} or \file{a.step(X, inverse=True)}. \file{verify=True} checks mass, candidate-key invariance, selected-key invariance, reversed orientations, and each block's involution. \file{trace=True} returns the final support and immutable step/block records. \file{a.E} and \file{a.P} expose candidates, eligibility, application, and local output. \file{a.gate_at(i)} materializes one template by stable global ID, and \file{a.encode(q,c0,c1,sign)} creates home encodings.

Position inputs must be exact built-in sets or frozensets whose elements are exact Python integers; Booleans, floats, and integer subclasses are rejected. Flags must be exact Booleans. Empty supports and arbitrary positive or negative integer coordinates are valid. Validation uses explicit exceptions, so it survives \file{python -O}. The source snapshot, compiled records, returned discovery/selection maps, and traces are immutable through ordinary attribute/container operations. Mutating the caller's source after compilation cannot change semantics. Low-level reflection and monkey-patching are outside this immutability contract. The fresh ordinary dictionary returned by \file{ledger()} may be modified without affecting the compiler.

Only source-derived immutable class guards are supported. Arbitrary caller-supplied executable predicates are not a public construction path. The bundled inherited source document describes its older API; the enclosing \file{SparseParallelCompiler} and the entry points above define this report's API.

\section{Measured evidence and independent checks}
\subsection{Small-source differential testing}
The frozen main suite executes 8,183 configurations over 10 sources and checks 1,140 arithmetic/eager template equalities. It records 16,366 comparisons/checks in each of the following categories: raw candidate maps, selected maps, block outputs, block involutions, whole-step equalities (forward and inverse combined), and whole-step roundtrips. Candidate equality includes exact invariant anchors and labels, not just final support or reversibility.

Coverage includes 2,280 endpoint orientations, 2,280 endpoint-plus-spoiler cases, 3,072 exhaustive malformed subsets over 11-site and 10-site universes, 192 encoded states, 120 random supports, 20 huge translations, 1,761 local-radius comparisons, and 162 guard-detector configurations. There are 259 API/mutation rejections and 19 source-validation parity checks. Thirty-two instrumented probe-bound cases include empty and singleton supports, prospective rejection, competition, valid states, and three separated copies. Maximum observed discovery counts are 8 without verification and 16 with it, within the mass-dependent bounds.

A 780,035-type source is compiled while eager construction and old-execution entry points are trapped. Compilation constructs zero endpoint templates; bounded runtime queries touch only five distinct types. The frozen main-suite elapsed times are 54.252 seconds normally and 50.512 seconds optimized. These machine-specific observations are not performance guarantees.

An independently authored all-index small-source oracle additionally checks 14 varied source cases, 17,504 contained-endpoint discoveries, 1,120 full raw key/orientation maps, 2,240 block candidate/selection/output comparisons, and 2,560 sparse/literal guard comparisons. Its final producer replay times are 3.796 and 4.156 seconds. The independent symbolic/code audit found no correctness defect; it is not a proof-assistant formalization.

\subsection{Frozen universal-source benchmark}
\wtag\ The source that Report~27 ships as \path{scientific/universal-source.json} is Report~17's (SHA-256 \texttt{fa61d06178d7\ldots}, Section~\ref{fpa:lz:sec:bytes}). It and the two universal traces of this benchmark are not shipped in this directory; the README gives the commands that rebuild all three byte for byte from shipped files in a few seconds.

The included 32,034,272-byte source has
\[
(m,p,a,J)=(122622,66066,75495,0),\quad D=509508,
\]
\[
F=269291358255,\qquad R=91711698.
\]
The benchmark disables old ordered execution and eager construction before metadata compilation. It runs 128 \emph{literal new-rule} startup steps with inverse verification and exact comparison against the frozen old admissible-state trace. It then runs 128 arbitrary endpoint/noise configurations sampled across the type range, including translation by $-2^{2048}$, with mass and roundtrip verification. No source-boundary jump is substituted for a CA step.

\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lrr@{}}
\toprule
Frozen producer measurement & Normal & Optimized \\ \midrule
Metadata compilation, seconds & 3.914 & 7.033 \\
128 literal startup steps, seconds & 0.136 & 0.194 \\
128 malformed cases, seconds & 0.118 & 0.168 \\
Total benchmark, seconds & 4.532 & 7.853 \\
Peak process RSS, KiB on Linux & 376,776 & 378,012 \\ \bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The bounded runs observe at most four candidate types, two raw keys, two selected keys, and two prospective probes per block. Optimization's longer compilation in this particular run reflects ordinary run variation, not different semantics. The benchmark imports the standard-library \file{resource} module, and its reported RSS units are Linux KiB.

These checks do not finish the long universal startup, execute a whole simulated Turing-machine step, establish halting, or materialize an eager universal oracle. The malformed benchmark supplies roundtrip corroboration; arbitrary-support equality rests on the proof and independent small-source comparisons. The frozen source's attributed universal construction is inherited rather than newly proved by this report.

\subsection{Frozen packet and release verification}
The published scientific subtree contains 39 files: 37 manifest entries plus its manifest and checksums. All scientific code, data, proofs, tests, and benchmark receipts are unchanged. Four inert metadata files are adapted for portable provenance: \file{PROVENANCE.json} uses relative packet origins; \file{audit-timings.json} names its interpreter as \file{python3}, retaining every flag and measured value; \file{manifest.json} and \file{SHA256SUMS} are regenerated. The other 35 files preserve their original bytes, modes, and modification times. Universal traces remain byte-identical to the producer.

The unchanged independent root review included with the references records proof/code inspection, both interpreter-mode replays, semantic receipt comparisons, and invariance of all 39 files in the \emph{original} scientific packet. It does not claim to review the later publication metadata or this article. The original scientific manifest is preserved as a pinned reference; its hash is distinguished from the published adapted manifest below. No executable scientific semantics changed.

The completed release adds exact-inventory verification and mandatory hardcoded source pins. Omitting the universal fixture is an error, not a reduced replay mode. Negative tests operate on disposable copies and exercise missing or added entries, unsafe metadata and paths, altered pins and dependencies, and symlinks. A byte-level privacy scan rejects known internal absolute-path prefixes; portable temporary-output examples remain allowed. Release replay uses six fresh relocated copies, one for each suite/mode combination, with external outputs, bytecode disabled, isolated Python, standard-library dependency checks, and a network audit guard. It checks the exact file and directory inventory, bytes, modes, and modification times after every subprocess.

Receipt comparison normalizes only declared timing/RSS fields and validated known optimization flags. All trace/hash/count fields are retained; universal trace bytes are compared exactly against the producer. A seal proves internal consistency, not authenticity against replacement of the verifier and all pins. The final delivery hashes provide an external reference.

\section{Attribution and arithmetic certification limits}
The two-block architecture belongs to established marker-automorphism and conserved-landscape traditions. Boyle, Lind, and Rudolph describe marker-delimited permutations and marker involutions~\cite{fpa:blr}; Toffoli and Margolus discuss conserved landscapes~\cite{fpa:tm}. Recognition-set preservation in Maldonado, Moreira, and Gajardo, Proposition 2, is a close proof precedent~\cite{fpa:mmg}. Their construction uses a different state space and does not itself supply this binary two-conservative-involution compiler. The equivalence between CA time symmetry and a product of two CA involutions is established in Gajardo, Kari, and Moreira~\cite{fpa:gkm}; reversibility alone is not that assertion.

The bundled bounded primary-source audit records exact checked passages and access limits. It does not establish priority, radius minimality, or global novelty. This report's contribution relative to the frozen construction is complete source-metadata-based discovery and exact sparse execution, together with the explicit probe and resource bounds.

An old arithmetic verifier for the ordered rule does not automatically certify the new map on malformed supports. Semantic equality transports admissible trajectories, but arbitrary new-rule certificates must encode complete raw discovery, all-type exclusion, hypothetical key-set preservation, and simultaneous writes. Two mathematical involutions do not mean two cheap arithmetic instructions. This release contains no new Diophantine compiler, no new universal equation, and no revised arithmetic gate count.

Useful next questions are to compile these exact finite-support predicates into a separately audited arithmetic verifier; account for sorting, integer encodings, and complete candidate witnesses; reduce explicit guard-class expansion where sound; and measure longer literal trajectories with clearly stated stopping conditions. They remain future work.

\wtag\ (3~October 2026.) The first of these questions is answered at each fixed horizon by Report~28 (Theorem~\ref{fpa:pq:thm:main}), whose emitter has an independent code audit and charges sorting (a bitonic network), signed integer encodings and complete candidate witnesses. Guard-class reduction and longer literal trajectories remain open.

No network, external sibling packet, or package installation is required. Allow roughly 370 MiB peak memory for the frozen benchmark, with headroom for process variation. python3 -B verify_release.py
python3 -B release_checks.py

\end{verbatim}
The chosen output directory must not exist and must be outside the release. Full replay automatically runs normal and optimized variants of the main differential suite, independent candidate audit, and universal benchmark. It replaces only the requested PDF and invalidates the existing release seal until the changed deliverable is reviewed and resealed.
